The balls $B_{\rho_i s}(y_i)$ are compactly contained in $U$ for large $i$. Applying Lemma \ref{L:monotonicity limit varifold} at the boundary point $y_i$ and using \eqref{E:Jacobian-estimate}, we obtain
\[
\Theta_V(y_i)
\leq e^{C_5\rho_i s}\frac{\|V\|(B_{\rho_i s}(y_i))}{\omega_{n-1}(\rho_i s)^{n-1}}
\leq e^{C_5\rho_i s}(1+o_i(1))
\frac{\|V_i\|(\overline{B^\delta_{(1+a)s}(q)})}{\omega_{n-1}s^{n-1}},
\]
where $o_i(1)\to0$ for fixed $s,a$. Weak convergence on the compact closed ball therefore yields
\[
\limsup_{i\to\infty}\Theta_V(y_i)
\leq\frac{\|\mathbf{C}\|(\overline{B^\delta_{(1+a)s}(q)})}{\omega_{n-1}s^{n-1}}.
\]
Letting first $s\downarrow0$ and then $a\downarrow0$ proves \eqref{E:upper-semi-continuity-density-1}, since open and closed balls give the same density limit.


\vspace{1ex}


\noindent \textit{Step 2: Decompose $\mathcal{S}_j\cap\partial\Omega\cap U$ into a countable union of sets satisfying the approximation property.}


\vspace{1ex}

This step is exactly the same as in the interior case. We provide the proof here for readers' convenience. First, for each $k \in \mathbb{N}$ we let
\begin{equation}
    \mathcal{S}_{j}^{k} := \left\{ x \in \mathcal{S}_j\cap\partial\Omega\cap U : \text{ \eqref{E:stratification-proof-key-claim} holds with $\overline{\rho}=\frac{1}{k}$} \right\},
\end{equation}
and for each $k,\ell \in \mathbb{N}$ we define
\begin{equation}
    \mathcal{S}_{j}^{k,\ell} := \left\{ x \in \mathcal{S}_{j}^{k}: \Theta_V(x) \in \left[ \frac{\ell-1}{k}, \frac{\ell}{k} \right) \right\}.
\end{equation}
Clearly, $\mathcal{S}_j\cap\partial\Omega\cap U=\bigcup_{k,\ell =1}^\infty \mathcal{S}_{j}^{k,\ell}$. For any $x \in \mathcal{S}_j^{k,\ell} \subset \mathcal{S}_j^k$, the definitions give
\[ \mathcal{S}_j^{k,\ell} \subset \left\{ y \in \mathcal{S}_j\cap\partial\Omega\cap U : \Theta_V(y) \geq \Theta_V(x)-\frac{1}{k} \right\},\]
and for every $\rho \in (0,\frac{1}{k}]$, there exists a $j$-dimensional subspace $W_{x,\rho} \subset T_x\partial\Omega$ such that
\[ \eta_{0,\rho} \left(\exp_x^{-1}\left( \left\{y \in B_\rho(x)\cap\partial\Omega:\Theta_V(y) \geq \Theta_V(x)-\frac{1}{k} \right\} \right) \right) \subset U_\epsilon(W_{x,\rho}). \]
Therefore,
\[ \eta_{0,\rho} ( \exp_x^{-1} (\mathcal{S}_j^{k,\ell} \cap B_\rho(x)) ) \subset U_\epsilon (W_{x,\rho}).\]

To pass to a fixed normal coordinate chart, let $f=\exp_p^{-1}$ and restrict to a relatively compact smaller chart on which $f$ and $f^{-1}$ have Lipschitz constants at most $L\geq1$. Smoothness gives
\[
f(y)-f(x)=df_x\bigl(\exp_x^{-1}(y)\bigr)+o(r)
\]
uniformly for $x,y$ in this smaller chart with $|f(y)-f(x)|<r$. Apply the preceding inclusion with $\rho=Lr\leq1/k$, and use $\|df_x\|\leq L$. For all sufficiently small $r$, depending also on $\epsilon$, the distance from $f(y)-f(x)$ to the $j$-dimensional subspace $df_x(W_{x,Lr})$ is strictly less than $2L^2\epsilon r$ whenever $x,y\in\mathcal{S}_j^{k,\ell}$ and $|f(y)-f(x)|<r$. Thus the coordinate image of each such local piece has the $2L^2\epsilon$-approximation property. The charts can be chosen with a common bound for $L$ by \eqref{E:metric-estimate} and \eqref{E:dexp-estimate}. A countable chart cover and the approximation lemma give
\[
\mathcal{H}^{j+\beta(2L^2\epsilon)}(\mathcal{S}_j\cap\partial\Omega\cap U)=0.
\]
Letting $\epsilon\downarrow0$ completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- remark-16: body_md -->
We point out that one needs to work within the class of integral varifolds to yield some useful geometric properties of curvature varifolds (with or without boundary). This is the setting considered in \cite{HutchinsonIUMJ} and \cite{Mantegazza1996}. One fundamental reason is that any integral varifolds with locally bounded first variation must have its generalized mean curvature vector perpendicular almost everywhere to the approximate tangent space (which exists a.e. by rectifiability). This result, known as Brakke's Perpendicularity Theorem, was first proved in the Euclidean setting by Brakke in his monumental monograph \cite[Chapter 5]{Brakke}. We shall extend this fundamental result to the intrinsic Riemannian setting. Note that this result is just about the properties of the generalized mean curvature vector but provides no information about the singular part $\|\delta V\|_{sing}$ of the first variation.
<!-- end remark-16 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{T:Brakke-perpendicularity}
Let $V \in \mathbf{IV}_{n-1}(U)$ be an integral varifold with locally bounded first variation. Then, we have
\[ V\Big( \{(x,S) \in G(U):S(H_V(x)) \neq 0 \} \Big) =0\]
where $H_V$ is the generalized mean curvature vector as defined in \eqref{E:Radon-Nikodym}. Moreover, the following ``flattening properties'' hold: for $\|V\|$-a.e. $x \in U$, 
\[ \lim_{r \to 0} \frac{1}{r^n} \int_{B_r(x)} \|P_{y,x}(T_y V)- T_x V\|^2 \; d\|V\|(y)=0  \]
where $P_{y,x}:\mathbf{G}_y(n,n-1) \to \mathbf{G}_x(n,n-1)$ is the map obtained by parallel transport from $y$ to $x$ along the unique minimizing geodesic between $y$ and $x$.
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- definition-5: body_md -->
\label{D:curvature-varifolds}
A varifold $V \in \mathbf{V}_{n-1}(U)$ is said to be a \emph{curvature varifold with boundary} if there exist on the Grassmannian $G(U)$
\begin{itemize}
\item a tensor-valued $\mathbf{B}_V \in L^1_{loc}(G(U);V)$ with values in $T^*\widetilde{M} \otimes T^* \widetilde{M} \otimes T \widetilde{M}$, i.e. for any continuous vector fields $X,Y$ on $G(U)$ with values in $T\widetilde{M}$, 
\[ \mathbf{B}_V(X,Y) \text{ is a locally $V$-integrable vector field with values in $T\widetilde{M}$}\]
satisfying  
\begin{equation}
\label{E:formal-properties-B}
V\left( \left\{ (x,S) \in G(U):S(\mathbf{B}_V (S^\perp(X), S^\perp (Y))  \neq 0 \text{ for some $X,Y$}  \right\}\right) =0;
\end{equation}
\item and a vector-valued Radon measure $\partial V$ on $G(U)$ with values in $T\widetilde{M}$, i.e. 
\[ \partial V= \textbf{n}_V \|\partial V\|\]
where $\|\partial V\|$ is the total variation Radon measure of $\partial V$ and $\textbf{n}_V$ is a $\|\partial V\|$-measurable vector field on $G(U)$ with values in $T\widetilde{M}$ and $|\textbf{n}_V|=1$ $\|\partial V\|$-a.e., satisfying
\begin{equation}
\label{E:formal-properties-partial-V}
\|\partial V\| \left( \left\{ (x,S) \in G(U):S(\textbf{n}_V)  \neq \textbf{n}_V   \right\}\right) =0;
\end{equation}
\end{itemize}
such that for any $\varphi \in C^1_c(G(U))$ and any $C^1$ vector field $X:U \to T\widetilde{M}$, we have
\[ \int_{G(U)} \varphi(x,S) \, X(x) \cdot \textbf{n}_V(x,S) d \|\partial V\|= \int_{G(U)} S(\nabla^{hor} \varphi(x,S)) \cdot X(x) +\varphi(x,S) \operatorname{div}_S X(x) \; dV(x,S)  \]
\begin{equation}
    \label{E:curvature-varifolds}
   +\int_{G(U)} \mathbf{B}_V(\nabla^{ver}\varphi(x,S),X(x)) \cdot N_S - \varphi(x,S) \, \mathbf{H}_V(x,S) \cdot X(x) \; dV(x,S)
\end{equation}
where $\mathbf{H}_V:=\operatorname{Tr} \mathbf{B}_V = \sum_{i=1}^{n} \mathbf{B}_V(e_i,e_i) \in L^1_{loc}(G(U);V)$ with values in $T\widetilde{M}$. Here, $\{e_i\}_{i=1}^n$ is any orthonormal basis for $T_x \widetilde{M}$ and $\nabla \varphi=\nabla^{hor} \varphi + \nabla^{ver} \varphi$ is the $\tilde{g}$-gradient of $\varphi$ written with respect to the orthogonal splitting \eqref{E:grassmann-splitting}. We call $\mathbf{B}_V$ the \emph{generalized second fundamental form}, $\textbf{n}_V$ the \emph{varifold outward co-normal} and $\|\partial V\|$ the \emph{varifold boundary measure} of $V$. Here, we adopt the convention to identify $T\widetilde{M}$ and $T^*\widetilde{M}$ using the metric $g$ whenever necessary. We shall use $\mathbf{CV}^\partial_{n-1}(U)$ to denote the space of all $(n-1)$-dimensional curvature varifolds with boundary in $U$.
<!-- end definition-5 -->
<!-- remark-17: body_md -->
Note that $\nabla^{ver} \varphi (x,S) \in \ker d\pi_{(x,S)}$, when regarded as a vector in $S$ is only well-defined up to the anti-podal isomorphism $p \mapsto -p$ on $S$. This reflects the choice of a unit normal $\pm N_S$ to the \emph{un-oriented} subspace $S$. Nonetheless, the term $\mathbf{B}_V(\nabla^{ver}\varphi(x,S),X(x)) \cdot N_S$ in \eqref{E:curvature-varifolds} is well-defined independent of the choice of $N_S$.
<!-- end remark-17 -->
<!-- remark-18: body_md -->
Condition \eqref{E:formal-properties-B} says that $\mathbf{B}_V$ at $V$-a.e. $(x,S) \in G(U)$ is essentially a bi-linear map $S \times S \to S^\perp$ (however, we do not assume that $\mathbf{B}_V(X,Y)= \mathbf{B}_V(Y,X)$ in general). Similarly, condition \eqref{E:formal-properties-partial-V} says that $\textbf{n}_V$ at $\|\partial V\|$-a.e. $(x,S)$ is essentially tangential to $S$. These conditions are necessary in order for $\mathbf{B}_V$ and $\textbf{n}_V$ to be uniquely defined by \eqref{E:curvature-varifolds}. Note that no such conditions are required in \cite{HutchinsonIUMJ} and \cite{Mantegazza1996} as they regard $\mathbf{G}_x(n,n-1)$ as a subset of $\mathbb{R}^{n^2}$, the space of all $n \times n$ real matrices, and they allow the test functions $\varphi \in C^1_c(U \times \mathbb{R}^{n^2})$ instead of the smaller subspace $C^1_c(G(U))$ considered here in \eqref{E:curvature-varifolds}. As a result, \eqref{E:formal-properties-B} and \eqref{E:formal-properties-partial-V} together imply all the formal and tangential properties in \cite[Proposition 3.6 and 3.7]{Mantegazza1996}.
<!-- end remark-18 -->
<!-- remark-19: body_md -->
\label{R:relation-Mantegazza}
    When $V \in \mathbf{IV}_{n-1}(U)$, our definition generalizes the notion of curvature varifolds with boundary in \cite{Mantegazza1996} to the case of general varifolds in the Riemannian setting. If $U \subset \mathbb{R}^n$, then the Grassmannian splits globally as a product $G(U) \subset U \times \mathfrak{gl}(n,\mathbb{R})$ if we regard $S \in \mathfrak{gl}(n,\mathbb{R}) \cong \mathbb{R}^{n^2}$ as the orthogonal projection matrix. Taking $X=\partial_i$ to be parallel coordinate vector fields in $\mathbb{R}^n$, it is straightforward to check that \eqref{E:curvature-varifolds} reduces to \cite[Definition 3.1]{Mantegazza1996}. In general, we can always locally trivialize the Grassmannian bundle $G(B_r(x))=B_r(x) \times \mathbf{G}_x(n,n-1)$, say within a small geodesic ball $B_r(x) \subset U$ and use parallel transport along minimizing geodesics from $x$ to identify $\mathbf{G}_y(n,n-1)$ with $\mathbf{G}_x(n,n-1)$ for all $y \in B_r(x)$, to reduce the situation to the Euclidean setting (modulo some extra terms coming from the ambient geometry). Notice that under such local trivialization, we can express any $\varphi \in C^1_c(G(B_r(x)))$ as $\varphi(y,S)$ where $y \in B_r(x)$ and $S \in \mathbf{G}_x(n,n-1)$ and the orthogonal splitting \ref{E:grassmann-splitting} at the base point $x \in \widetilde{M}$ becomes
    \[ \nabla \varphi (x,S)= \nabla^{\widetilde{M}} \varphi (x,S) + \nabla^{\mathbf{G}_x(n,n-1)} \varphi (x,S).\]
    Note that the extra term $\operatorname{div}_S X$ appearing in \eqref{E:curvature-varifolds} (that was not present in \cite{Mantegazza1996}), when $X=\partial_i$ is a (non-parallel!) coordinate vector field in geodesic normal coordinates, involves up to first order derivatives of $g_{ij}$ and can be estimated using \eqref{E:metric-estimate}.
<!-- end remark-19 -->
<!-- remark-20: body_md -->
\label{R:curvature-varifold-local}
    Note that the notion of curvature varifold with boundary is essentially a local property in the sense that one only needs to check \eqref{E:curvature-varifolds} for $\varphi \in C^1_c(G(U_\alpha))$ where $\{U_\alpha\}_{\alpha}$ is any locally finite open cover of $U$. This follows from the linearity of $\varphi$ in \eqref{E:curvature-varifolds} and a standard partition of unity argument. We will mainly apply this observation to any locally finite open cover of $U$ by small enough geodesic balls.
<!-- end remark-20 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{L:curvaure-varifolds-definition}
Let $V \in \mathbf{CV}^\partial_{n-1}(U) \cap \mathbf{RV}_{n-1}(U)$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] $\mathbf{B}_V$ and $\partial V$ are uniquely determined by \eqref{E:curvature-varifolds} and $\operatorname{spt} \|\partial V\| \subset \operatorname{spt} V$.
\item[(ii)] If $V \in \mathbf{IV}_{n-1}(U)$, then $\pi_* \|\partial V\|$ is singular with respect to $\|V\|$, i.e. 
\[ \pi_* \|\partial V\| \perp \|V\|.\]
Moreover, $V$ has locally bounded first variation in $U$ and the generalized mean curvature $H_V$ and the generalized vector-valued boundary measure $\eta_V \, \|\delta V\|_{sing}$ defined in \eqref{E:Radon-Nikodym} are given by
\begin{equation}
\label{E:relation-to-first-variation}    
H_V(x)=\mathbf{H}_V(x,T_xV) \text{ and } \eta_V \|\delta V\|_{sing}=\pi_*(\partial V)
\end{equation} 
where $T_xV$ is the approximate tangent space of $V$ which exists for $\|V\|$-a.e. $x \in U$.


\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
For (i), the proof in \cite[Proposition 3.4]{Mantegazza1996} carries over once we fix a local trivialization as in Remark \ref{R:relation-Mantegazza} and choose $\varphi(y,S)=\phi(y)\psi(S)$ and $X=\partial_i$. It is clear from \eqref{E:curvature-varifolds} that $\operatorname{spt} \|\partial V\| \subset \operatorname{spt} V$. Note that the proof in \cite{Mantegazza1996} only requires rectifiability of $V$. For (ii), if we take $\varphi=\varphi(x) \in C^1_c(U)$ (or more precisely, the lift of such a function to $G(U)$), using Theorem \ref{T:Brakke-perpendicularity} and \eqref{E:formal-properties-partial-V}, we see that $V$ has locally bounded first variation and \eqref{E:relation-to-first-variation} holds (c.f. \cite[p.827--829]{Mantegazza1996}).
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- remark-21: body_md -->
If $V \in \mathbf{RV}_{n-1}(U)$ is not integral, then $\pi_* \|\partial V\|$ is not necessarily singular with respect to $\|V\|$ since $\partial V$ will also contain information about the tangential derivative of the density $\Theta_V$. See Example \ref{Ex:generalised-sff-smooth}.
<!-- end remark-21 -->
<!-- example-2: body_md -->
Consider the unit-density varifold $V \in \mathbf{IV}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^2)$ given by three rays from the origin $\ell^+_1, \ell^+_2, \ell^+_3$ meeting at equal angle of $2\pi/3$. Denote by $\vec{n}_i$ the unit vector centred at the origin pointing towards $\ell_i$. Due to the balancing of $\vec{n}_i$ at the origin, $V$ is a stationary varifold in $\mathbb{R}^2$, hence $H_V \equiv 0$ and $\|\delta V\|_{sing}=0$. One can check from the definition that $V$ is a curvature varifold with boundary with $\mathbf{B}_V \equiv 0$ and
\[ \partial V = \sum_{i=1}^3 \vec{n}_i \delta_{(0,\ell_i)} \neq 0 \]
where $\ell_i \in \mathbf{G}_0(2,1)$ is the unique line containing the ray $\ell^+_i$. Note that $\pi_* (\partial V)= \left(\sum_{i=1}^3 \vec{n}_i\right) \delta_0=0$. On the other hand, $\|\partial V\|=\sum_{i=1}^3 \delta_{(0,\ell_i)}$ is a sum of three delta measures on the Grassmannian $G(\mathbb{R}^2)$ and the pushforward measure $\pi_* \|\partial V\|=3 \delta_0$ is singular with respect to $\|V\|$. This example shows that in general we only have $\|\delta V\|_{sing} \leq \pi_* \|\partial V\|$ as Radon measures on $U$.
<!-- end example-2 -->
<!-- example-3: body_md -->
\label{Ex:generalised-sff-smooth}
Let $\Sigma \subset \mathbb{R}^n$ be a properly embedded smooth hypersurface with boundary and $f:\Sigma \to \mathbb{R}$ be a smooth function. Suppose $\Sigma$ is two-sided and oriented by the global unit normal vector field $N_\Sigma$. Consider the rectifiable varifold $V \in \mathbf{RV}_{n-1}(\mathbb{R}^n)$ with weight measure given by
\[ \|V\|=e^f d\mathcal{H}^{n-1} \mathbin{\vrule height 1.6ex depth 0pt width
0.13ex\vrule height 0.13ex depth 0pt width 1.3ex} \Sigma,\]
then a direct computation shows that $V$ is a curvature varifold with boundary with $\mathbf{B}_V:\mathbb{R}^n \times \mathbb{R}^n \to \mathbb{R}^n$ supported on $T\Sigma$ given by the classical second fundamental form\footnote{We denote $w=w^T+w^N$ for the orthogonal decomposition $T_x \mathbb{R}^n=T_x \Sigma \oplus N_x \Sigma$}: 
\[ \mathbf{B}_V|_{(x,T_x \Sigma)}(u,v):= (D_{u^T} v^T)^N,\]
and the vector-valued Radon measure $\partial V$ is given by
\[ \partial V(x,T_x \Sigma)= -e^f\nabla^\Sigma f (x) \, d \mathcal{H}^{n-1} \mathbin{\vrule height 1.6ex depth 0pt width
0.13ex\vrule height 0.13ex depth 0pt width 1.3ex} \Sigma (x) \otimes \delta_{T_x\Sigma} + e^f\eta(x) \, d\mathcal{H}^{n-2} \mathbin{\vrule height 1.6ex depth 0pt width
0.13ex\vrule height 0.13ex depth 0pt width 1.3ex} \partial \Sigma (x) \otimes \delta_{T_x\Sigma}\]
where $\eta(x) \in T_x\Sigma$ is the outward unit co-normal of $\partial \Sigma$. Note that $\partial V$ contains a non-trivial part which is absolutely continuous with respect to $V$ unless $f$ is constant on $\Sigma$. On the other hand, $V$ has locally bounded first variation with $H_V(x)=\mathbf{H}_V(x,T_x\Sigma)$ and
\[ \|\delta V\|_{sing}= e^fd\mathcal{H}^{n-2} \mathbin{\vrule height 1.6ex depth 0pt width
0.13ex\vrule height 0.13ex depth 0pt width 1.3ex} \partial \Sigma \leq e^f|\nabla^\Sigma f| \, d \mathcal{H}^{n-1} \mathbin{\vrule height 1.6ex depth 0pt width
0.13ex\vrule height 0.13ex depth 0pt width 1.3ex} \Sigma + e^fd\mathcal{H}^{n-2} \mathbin{\vrule height 1.6ex depth 0pt width
0.13ex\vrule height 0.13ex depth 0pt width 1.3ex} \partial \Sigma = \pi_* \|\partial V\|. \]
More generally, one can also consider $f \in BV(\Sigma)$ and replace $\nabla^\Sigma f  \, d \mathcal{H}^{n-1} \mathbin{\vrule height 1.6ex depth 0pt width
0.13ex\vrule height 0.13ex depth 0pt width 1.3ex} \Sigma$ with the vector-valued Radon measure $Df$ on $\Sigma$.
<!-- end example-3 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{T:boundary-rectifiability}
     Let $V \in \mathbf{CV}^\partial_{n-1}(U) \cap \mathbf{IV}_{n-1}(U)$. Then, $\pi_* \|\partial V\|$ is an $(n-2)$-rectifiable measure on $U$, i.e. there exists a countably $(n-2)$-rectifiable subset $\Gamma \subset U$ and a positive Borel function $\tau :\Gamma \to (0,\infty)$ such that 
     \[ \pi_* \|\partial V\|=\tau \; d\mathcal{H}^{n-2}\mathbin{\vrule height 1.6ex depth 0pt width
0.13ex\vrule height 0.13ex depth 0pt width 1.3ex} \Gamma.\]
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- remark-22: body_md -->
\label{R:varifold-definition}
	Our definition of the varifold $V^{u_\varepsilon}$ in \eqref{E:varifold-def} is consistent with \cite{Hiesmayr2017, Chodosh2020, Mantoulidis2021}, but slightly different from the one in \cite{LiPariseSarnataro2023}. Nonetheless, whenever \textit{equi-partition of energy} holds (i.e. $\xi_{\varepsilon} \to 0$ in $L^1(U)$), it will give rise to the same varifold limit $V^{u_\varepsilon} \to V$ if it exists as $\varepsilon\to 0$.
<!-- end remark-22 -->
<!-- assumptions-1: body_md -->
\label{As:stationary-AC}
We will consider solutions $u_\varepsilon$ to \eqref{E:Allen-Cahn} under the following additional assumptions:
\begin{itemize}
    \item[(A1)] (Uniform energy bound) There exists some $E_0>0$ such that
    \[ E_{\varepsilon}(u_\varepsilon) \leq E_0.\]
    \item[(A2)] (Uniform $L^\infty$ bound on $u_\varepsilon$) There exists some $C_0>0$ such that
    \[ \|u_\varepsilon\|_{L^\infty (\Omega \cap U)} \leq C_0.\]
                                                                                                                                
                                                                             
\end{itemize}
<!-- end assumptions-1 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
{\cite[Theorem 3.3]{LiPariseSarnataro2023}}
\label{T:old-main}
Let $\{\varepsilon_i\}_{i=1}^\infty$ be a sequence of positive real numbers such that $\varepsilon_i\to 0$, and let $\{u_{\varepsilon_i}\}_{i=1}^\infty$ be a sequence of solutions to \eqref{E:Allen-Cahn} satisfying (A1)-(A2) in Assumptions \ref{As:stationary-AC} with respect to a sequence of Riemannian metrics $g_i$ on $U$ and a sequence of domains $\Omega_i \subset \widetilde{M}$ such that
\[ g_i \to g \quad \text{ and } \quad \Omega_i \to \Omega\]
in the $C^2$-sense to some Riemannian metric $g$ and some $C^2$ domain $\Omega \subset \widetilde{M}$ satisfying all the assumptions in \ref{SS:setup}.
                       
                                                                                                                                                                                                                    
                                                                          
                                                                       
                
Then, after passing to a subsequence, there exist some $u \in BV(\Omega \cap U)$ and $V \in \mathbf{V}_{n-1}(U)$ such that
\begin{itemize}
                                                                      
\item $u_{\varepsilon_i} \to u$ strongly in $L^1(\Omega \cap U;d\mathrm{vol}_g)$; 
\item $V^{u_{\epsilon_i}} \to V$ as varifolds in $U$ (recall \eqref{E:varifold-def}).
\end{itemize}
Moreover, the following statements are true for any such $u$ and $V$ obtained above:
\begin{itemize}
\item[(1)] (Discrepancy estimates and equi-partition of energy) The discrepancy function $\xi_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i})$ as defined in \eqref{E:energy-density-discrepancy} satisfies the pointwise upper bound locally away from $\partial U$:
\begin{equation}
    \label{E:discrepancy-pointwise-bound}
    \xi_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i}) \leq \varepsilon_i^{-4/5} \qquad \text{ for all large $i$};
\end{equation}
moreover the discrepancy function converges to zero strongly in $L^1$, i.e.
\begin{equation}
    \label{E:vanishing-discrepancy-limit}
    \xi_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i}) \to 0 \quad \text{ in } L^1(\Omega \cap U;d\mathrm{vol}_g).
\end{equation}
As a result, we have for each $\phi \in C_c(U)$,
\begin{equation}
\label{E:equipartition energy}
\|V\|(\phi)= \lim_{i \to \infty} \mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}(\phi)= \lim_{i \to \infty} \frac{2}{h_0} \int_{\overline{\Omega} \cap U} \frac{W(u_{\varepsilon_i})}{\varepsilon_i} \phi \; d\mathrm{vol}_g.
\end{equation}
\item[(2)] (Hausdorff convergence of level sets) The phase boundary is contained in the support of the limit-interface, i.e. 
\[ \partial^* \{u=1\} \subset \operatorname{spt} \|V\| \]
where $\partial^* \{u=1\} \subset \Omega \cap U$ is the reduced boundary\footnote{See, for example, \cite{Koltai-Volkner} for a precise definition in the local Riemannian setting.} of $u \in BV(\Omega \cap U)$. Moreover, as $i \to \infty$,
\[ u_{\varepsilon_i} \to \pm 1 \quad \text{ locally uniformly on $(\Omega \cap U) \setminus \operatorname{spt} \|V\|$}\]
and for each $b \in (0,1)$, 
\[ \{x \in \overline{\Omega} \cap U : |u_{\varepsilon_i}(x)| \leq 1-b\} \to \operatorname{spt} \|V\| \quad  \text{ locally in Hausdorff topology.}\]
\item[(3)] (Integer-rectifiability of limit-interface) The limit-interface is integer-rectifiable, i.e. $V \in \mathbf{IV}_{n-1}(U)$, with density 
\[ \Theta_V(x)= \left\{ \begin{array}{cl}
\text{odd} & \text{for $\mathcal{H}^{n-1}$-a.e. $x \in \partial^* \{u=1\}$},\\
\text{even} & \text{for $\mathcal{H}^{n-1}$-a.e. $x \in (\operatorname{spt} \|V\| \cap \Omega) \setminus \partial^* \{u=1\}$}.
\end{array} \right.
\]
                                                                                                                      
\item[(4)] (Variational property of limit-interface) The limit-interface $V \in \mathbf{IV}_{n-1}(U)$ is free boundary stationary, with respect to the metric $g$, with free boundary in $\partial \Omega \cap U$ and $\operatorname{spt} \|V\| \subset \overline{\Omega} \cap U$.
                                                                      
                                                                                                                             
                                                       
                               
                                                                                                             
                                                                                                                   
                                                                                              
                     
\end{itemize}
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- remark-23: body_md -->
\label{R:old-theorem-optimal}
	The assumptions (A1) and (A2) are really the minimal assumptions one has to make to expect the conclusions in Theorem \ref{T:old-main}. Condition (A2) typically follows from a maximum principle argument on the global Allen-Cahn equation \eqref{E:Allen-Cahn-intro}. Condition (A1) is necessary in order to guarantee a uniform bound on the BV-norm of $u_{\varepsilon_i}$ and the mass of $V^{u_{\varepsilon_i}}$ so that standard compactness theorems can be applied to extract a convergent subsequence. In particular, there are no assumptions on the stability (or Morse index) of $u_{\varepsilon_i}$ with respect to the Allen-Cahn energy functional $E_{\varepsilon_i}$. We emphasize that Theorem \ref{T:old-main} allows higher multiplicity convergence and boundary concentration phenomena as constructed by Malchiodi-Ni-Wei \cite{Malchiodi2007a} and Malchiodi-Wei \cite{Malchiodi2007}. As these examples show, in general the limit-interface $V$ may not be stationary in $U$ if there is a part of $\operatorname{spt} \|V\|$ leaning onto the boundary $\partial \Omega$. Nonetheless, the first variation of $V$ is given by \eqref{E:1st-variation-fbv-stationary} but it is a difficult question to infer further regularity (e.g. whether $\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\operatorname{sing} V)=0$) as a-priori the touching part can interact with $\|\delta V\|_{sing}$ in highly complicated manners.
<!-- end remark-23 -->
<!-- remark-24: body_md -->
\label{R:up-to-boundary-Hausdorff-convergence}
    Note that we have only established in \cite{LiPariseSarnataro2023} Hausdorff convergence of the transition level sets to the support of the limit-interface \emph{locally away from $\partial \Omega$}. This is due to the technical arguments that in \cite{LiPariseSarnataro2023} we only make use of monotonicity formula on balls that are either centred at a boundary point or disjoint from $\partial \Omega$. The last assertion of (2) in Theorem \ref{T:old-main} essentially relies on a uniform density-ratio bounds which have to be also valid for balls that intersect $\partial \Omega$ but are not centred at a boundary point. This can be achieved by a reflected ball argument as in \cite{Grueter1986} and \cite{Tonegawa2003}. For the sake of completeness, we provide the proof in Appendix \ref{A:reflected-density}.
<!-- end remark-24 -->
<!-- assumptions-2: body_md -->
\label{As:stable-AC}
From this section onwards, we will consider solutions $u_\varepsilon$ to \eqref{E:Allen-Cahn} under the following additional assumption:
\begin{itemize}
    \item[(A3)] (Stability) Each $u_\varepsilon$ is a stable critical point in the sense that the second variation of $E_\varepsilon$ is non-negative-definite at $u_\varepsilon$, i.e.
\begin{equation}
\label{E:def-stability}
	\left. \frac{d^2}{dt^2} \right\vert_{t = 0} E_\varepsilon(u_\varepsilon + t\psi)\geq 0 \qquad \forall \psi \in C_c^1(U).
\end{equation}
\end{itemize}
<!-- end assumptions-2 -->
<!-- proposition-5: body_md -->
\label{P:ep-stability}
    Suppose $u_\varepsilon$ is a stable solution to \eqref{E:Allen-Cahn} satisfying (A3) in Assumption \ref{As:stable-AC}. Then the following inequality holds: for any $\psi \in C^1_c(U)$,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{E:ep-stability}
        \int |\mathcal{A}_{u_\varepsilon}|^2 \psi^2 \; d\mu_{u_\varepsilon} \leq \int |\nabla \psi|^2 + \operatorname{Ric} ( N_{u_\varepsilon},N_{u_\varepsilon}) \psi^2  \; d\mu_{u_\varepsilon}  + \int \mathrm{I\!I}_{\partial \Omega} (N_{u_\varepsilon},N_{u_\varepsilon}) \psi^2 \; d\mu^\partial_{u_\varepsilon}
    \end{equation}
    where $\operatorname{Ric}$ is the Ricci curvature of $(\widetilde{M},g)$ and $\mathrm{I\!I}_{\partial \Omega}$ is the second fundamental form\footnote{Our sign convention here is that $\mathrm{I\!I}_{\partial \Omega}$ is positive definite for a convex domain $\Omega \subset (\widetilde{M},g)$.} of $\partial \Omega$ with respect to the outward unit normal $\nu$.
<!-- end proposition-5 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
A direct calculation as in \cite{Padilla1998} and integrating by parts gives for any $\phi \in C^1_c(U)$,
    \begin{equation}
    \label{E:2nd-variation-boundary} 
	\begin{split}
    	0 \leq \left. \frac{d^2}{dt^2} \right\vert_{t = 0} E_\varepsilon(u_\varepsilon + t\phi) & = \int_{\Omega\cap U} \left( \varepsilon \vert \nabla \phi \vert^2 + \frac{1}{\varepsilon} W^{\prime \prime}(u_\varepsilon) \phi^2 \right) \, d\mathrm{vol}_g, \\
    	& = \int_{\Omega \cap U} \phi\left( - \varepsilon \Delta \phi + \frac{1}{\varepsilon} W^{\prime \prime}(u_\varepsilon) \phi \right) d\mathrm{vol}_g + \frac{\varepsilon}{2} \int_{\partial \Omega \cap U} \frac{\partial \phi^2}{\partial \nu} \, d\mathcal{H}^{n-1} \\
    	                                                                                                                       
	\end{split}
\end{equation}
To rewrite the above in the form of \eqref{E:ep-stability}, first recall by the Bochner formula that 
\begin{equation}
\label{E:Bochner-u-ep}
    \frac{\varepsilon}{2}\Delta \vert \nabla u_\varepsilon \vert^2 = \varepsilon\vert \operatorname{Hess} u_\varepsilon \vert^2 + \frac{1}{\varepsilon} W^{\prime \prime}(u_\varepsilon) \vert \nabla u_\varepsilon \vert^2 + \varepsilon \operatorname{Ric} (\nabla u_\varepsilon, \nabla u_\varepsilon)
\end{equation}
and also the boundary analogue of Bochner formula (c.f. \cite[Lemma 3.1]{Tonegawa2003})
\begin{equation}
\label{E:Bochner-u-ep-boundary}
    \frac{\partial |\nabla u_\varepsilon|^2}{\partial \nu} = - 2 \mathrm{I\!I}_{\partial \Omega}(\nabla u_\varepsilon, \nabla u_\varepsilon).  
\end{equation}
We can then\footnote{Although \eqref{E:2nd-variation-boundary} was derived for $C^1$ test functions $\phi$, it is standard to see by an approximation argument that our choice $\psi |\nabla u_\varepsilon|$ is a valid test function even though $|\nabla u_\varepsilon|$ is in general not $C^1$ across the exceptional set $\{|\nabla u_\varepsilon|=0\}$. The interested readers can refer to \cite[Proposition 2.6]{Padilla1998} for more details. Also, technically $\psi |\nabla u_\varepsilon|$ is only defined on $\overline{\Omega} \cap U$ but we can extend it suitably across $\partial \Omega \cap U$ which does not affect the integrals in \eqref{E:2nd-variation-boundary}.} plug $\phi=\psi |\nabla u_\varepsilon|$ into \eqref{E:2nd-variation-boundary} to obtain \eqref{E:ep-stability}.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{T:main-AC}
Let $\{\varepsilon_i\}_{i=1}^\infty$ be a sequence of positive real numbers such that $\varepsilon_i\to 0$, and let $\{u_{\varepsilon_i}\}_{i=1}^\infty$ be a sequence of solutions to \eqref{E:Allen-Cahn} satisfying (A1)-(A2) in Assumptions \ref{As:stationary-AC}. 
Suppose, furthermore, that each $u_{\varepsilon_i}$ is stable, i.e. (A3) in Assumption \ref{As:stable-AC} is satisfied, with respect to the metric $g_i$. Then, for any limit-interface $V \in \mathbf{IV}_{n-1}(U)$ obtained from Theorem \ref{T:old-main}, the following holds:
\begin{enumerate}
\item $V$ is a curvature varifold with boundary (recall Definition \ref{D:curvature-varifolds}), i.e. 
\[ V \in \mathbf{IV}_{n-1}(U) \cap \mathbf{CV}^\partial_{n-1}(U).\]
\item (Ambient stability inequality) For any $\psi \in C^1_c(U)$, we have
\begin{equation}
\label{E:stability-inequality-boundary}
    \int |\mathbf{B}_V|^2\psi^2 \, d\|V\| \leq \int |\nabla\psi|^2 \, d\|V\| + (n-1) k_0 \int \psi^2 \, d\|V\| + 2 \kappa_0 \int \psi^2 \, d \|\delta V\|_{sing},
\end{equation}
where $|\mathbf{B}_V|^2(x)=|\mathbf{B}_V(x,T_x V)|^2$ with $T_x V$ being the approximate tangent plane of $\|V\|$ that exists at $\|V\|$-a.e. $x \in U$.
\item (Stationarity across touching set) The first variation of $V$ is given by
\begin{equation}
\label{E:minimal-touching}
    \delta V(X)= \int X \cdot \nu \; d \|\delta V\|_{sing} \qquad \forall X \in \mathfrak{X}_c(U).
\end{equation}
\end{enumerate}

\begin{remark}
    By (1) and Theorem \ref{T:boundary-rectifiability}, $\pi_* \|\partial V\|$ is an $(n-2)$-rectifiable measure on $U$.
\end{remark}
                                                                    
\begin{remark}
Note that if $V=|\Sigma|$ is a unit-density varifold for some smoothly embedded, two-sided, stable free boundary minimal hypersurface $\Sigma \subset \Omega$ with $\partial \Sigma \subset \partial \Omega$, the classical \emph{intrinsic} stability inequality for $\Sigma$ reads (recall the notation as in Example \ref{Ex:generalised-sff-smooth}):
\[ \int_\Sigma |B_\Sigma|^2 \psi^2 \; d\mathcal{H}^{n-1} \leq \int_\Sigma |\nabla^\Sigma \psi|^2 \; d\mathcal{H}^{n-1} - \int_\Sigma \operatorname{Ric} (N_\Sigma,N_\Sigma)  \psi^2 \; d\mathcal{H}^{n-1} - \int_{\partial \Sigma} \mathrm{I\!I}_{\partial \Omega}(N_\Sigma,N_\Sigma) \psi^2 \; d \mathcal{H}^{n-2} \]
where $B_\Sigma$ is the classical second fundamental form of $\Sigma$. Comparing this with \eqref{E:stability-inequality-boundary}, note that the term involving the extrinsic gradient $\nabla \psi$ is replaced by the intrinsic gradient $\nabla^\Sigma \psi$ and the boundary term differs by a factor of two.   
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}
\label{R:stationarity-across-touching}
Since $V$ is a free boundary stationary varifold with free boundary in $\partial \Omega \cap U$ and $\operatorname{spt} \|V\| \subset \overline{\Omega} \cap U$, combining \eqref{E:minimal-touching} with \eqref{E:1st-variation-fbv-stationary}, we conclude that
\begin{equation}
\label{E:minimal-touching-2}
    H_{\partial \Omega}(x)=0 \qquad \text{for $\|V\|$-a.e. $x \in \partial \Omega \cap U$.}
\end{equation}
In other words, the part that $V$ leans on to the boundary must be a minimal portion of $\partial \Omega$.
\end{remark}
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- remark-25: body_md -->
By (1) and Theorem \ref{T:boundary-rectifiability}, $\pi_* \|\partial V\|$ is an $(n-2)$-rectifiable measure on $U$.
<!-- end remark-25 -->
<!-- remark-26: body_md -->
Note that if $V=|\Sigma|$ is a unit-density varifold for some smoothly embedded, two-sided, stable free boundary minimal hypersurface $\Sigma \subset \Omega$ with $\partial \Sigma \subset \partial \Omega$, the classical \emph{intrinsic} stability inequality for $\Sigma$ reads (recall the notation as in Example \ref{Ex:generalised-sff-smooth}):
\[ \int_\Sigma |B_\Sigma|^2 \psi^2 \; d\mathcal{H}^{n-1} \leq \int_\Sigma |\nabla^\Sigma \psi|^2 \; d\mathcal{H}^{n-1} - \int_\Sigma \operatorname{Ric} (N_\Sigma,N_\Sigma)  \psi^2 \; d\mathcal{H}^{n-1} - \int_{\partial \Sigma} \mathrm{I\!I}_{\partial \Omega}(N_\Sigma,N_\Sigma) \psi^2 \; d \mathcal{H}^{n-2} \]
where $B_\Sigma$ is the classical second fundamental form of $\Sigma$. Comparing this with \eqref{E:stability-inequality-boundary}, note that the term involving the extrinsic gradient $\nabla \psi$ is replaced by the intrinsic gradient $\nabla^\Sigma \psi$ and the boundary term differs by a factor of two.
<!-- end remark-26 -->
<!-- remark-27: body_md -->
\label{R:stationarity-across-touching}
Since $V$ is a free boundary stationary varifold with free boundary in $\partial \Omega \cap U$ and $\operatorname{spt} \|V\| \subset \overline{\Omega} \cap U$, combining \eqref{E:minimal-touching} with \eqref{E:1st-variation-fbv-stationary}, we conclude that
\begin{equation}
\label{E:minimal-touching-2}
    H_{\partial \Omega}(x)=0 \qquad \text{for $\|V\|$-a.e. $x \in \partial \Omega \cap U$.}
\end{equation}
In other words, the part that $V$ leans on to the boundary must be a minimal portion of $\partial \Omega$.
<!-- end remark-27 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
First of all, we show that that the first variation of the diffused varifold $V^{u_{\varepsilon}} \in \mathbf{V}_{n-1}(U)$ (recall \eqref{E:varifold-def}) associated to a solution $u_{\varepsilon}$ to \eqref{E:Allen-Cahn} is given by: for any $\tilde{X} \in \mathfrak{X}_c(U)$,
\begin{equation}
\label{E:diffused-V-first-variation}
		\delta V^{u_\varepsilon} (\tilde{X}) =\int_{\Omega \cap U}\xi_\varepsilon(u_\varepsilon)\operatorname{div}_g \tilde{X} \; d\mathrm{vol}_g+\int_{\partial \Omega \cap U} e_\varepsilon(u_\varepsilon) (\tilde{X} \cdot \nu) \; d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}.
	\end{equation}
To prove \eqref{E:diffused-V-first-variation}, we first recall the following Pohozaev identity (see \cite[Lemma 4.1]{LiPariseSarnataro2023}, note that we are using the normalized energy density as in \eqref{E:energy-density-discrepancy})
	\begin{equation}\label{E:Pohozaev-1} 
		\int_{\Omega \cap U} \left( h_0 e_\varepsilon(u_\varepsilon)\operatorname{div}_g \tilde{X} - \varepsilon \nabla \tilde{X} (\nabla u_\varepsilon, \nabla u_\varepsilon) \right) \; d\mathrm{vol}_g 
		=\int_{\partial \Omega \cap U} h_0 e_\varepsilon(u_\varepsilon) (\tilde{X} \cdot \nu) \; d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}.
	\end{equation}
Observe that $\operatorname{div}_{S^{u_\varepsilon}} \tilde{X} = \operatorname{div}_g \tilde{X} - \nabla \tilde{X} (N_{u_\varepsilon},N_{u_\varepsilon})$ and $\varepsilon |\nabla u_\varepsilon|^2 = h_0 (e_\varepsilon(u_\varepsilon)+\xi_\varepsilon(u_\varepsilon))$, the integrand in the left hand side of \eqref{E:Pohozaev-1} can be written as
\[ h_0 e_\varepsilon(u_\varepsilon)\operatorname{div}_g \tilde{X} - \varepsilon \nabla \tilde{X} (\nabla u_\varepsilon, \nabla u_\varepsilon)= - h_0 \xi_\varepsilon(u_\varepsilon) \operatorname{div}_g \tilde X + \varepsilon |\nabla u_\varepsilon|^2 \operatorname{div}_{S^{u_\varepsilon}} \tilde{X} \]
from which \eqref{E:diffused-V-first-variation} follows (recall \eqref{E:varifold-def}).

Next, we test \eqref{E:diffused-V-first-variation} with a vector field\footnote{Strictly speaking, in order to deal with the singularities of $N_\varepsilon$ across $\{|\nabla u_\varepsilon|=0\}$, we should first consider $\varphi\left(x,Id-\frac{\nabla u_\varepsilon\otimes\nabla u_\varepsilon}{s+|\nabla u_\varepsilon|^2}\right)$ for $s>0$ and then let $s \to 0$. For more details on this technical aspect, see \cite{Tonegawa2005a}. For simplicity of presentation, we will pretend $|\nabla u|\neq 0$ everywhere.}
\[ \tilde{X}(x)=\varphi(x,S^{u_\varepsilon}(x)) S^{u_\varepsilon}(x)(X(x))\]
for any $C^1$ vector field $X:U \to T\widetilde{M}$ and any $\varphi \in C^1_c(G(B_r(x_0)))$ where $x_0 \in U$ and $r<\frac{1}{4}\min\{\operatorname{dist}_g(x_0,\partial U), i_0\}$. A direct computation shows that
\[ \operatorname{div}_{S^{u_\varepsilon}} \tilde{X}=S^{u_\varepsilon}(\nabla^{hor} \varphi) \cdot X + \varphi \operatorname{div}_{S^{u_\varepsilon}} X + B_{u_\varepsilon}(\nabla^{ver} \varphi, S^{u_\varepsilon}(X)) \cdot N_{u_\varepsilon} - \varphi H_{u_\varepsilon} \cdot X \]
where everything is evaluated at either $x$ or $(x,S^{u_\varepsilon}(x))$; here $B_{u_\varepsilon}(x)$ and $H_{u_\varepsilon}(x)$ are respectively the second fundamental form (as a $(1,2)$-tensor) and the mean curvature vector (see Example \ref{Ex:generalised-sff-smooth}) of the level set $\{u_\varepsilon=u_\varepsilon(x)\}$ of $u_\varepsilon$ through the point $x \in \overline{\Omega} \cap U$. Note that the free boundary condition $\frac{\partial u_\varepsilon}{\partial \nu}=0$ means that
\[ S^{u_\varepsilon}(x)(\nu(x))=\nu(x) \quad \text{for all $x \in \partial \Omega$.} \]
Plugging these back into \eqref{E:diffused-V-first-variation}, we obtain
\[ \int_{\partial \Omega \cap U} \varphi(x,S^{u_\varepsilon}(x)) X(x) \cdot \nu(x) e_\varepsilon(u_\varepsilon)(x) \, d\mathcal{H}^{n-1} + \int_{\Omega \cap U} \xi_\varepsilon(u_\varepsilon)(x) \operatorname{div}_g \tilde{X}(x) \, d\mathrm{vol}_g  \]    
\begin{equation}
\label{E:ep-curvature-varifold}
    =\int_{\Omega \cap U} S^{u_\varepsilon}(x)\left(\nabla^{hor} \varphi (x,S^{u_\varepsilon}(x)) \right) \cdot X(x) + \varphi(x,S^{u_\varepsilon}(x)) \operatorname{div}_{S^{u_\varepsilon}}X(x) \, dV^{u_\varepsilon}
\end{equation}
\[ + \int_{\Omega \cap U} B_{u_\varepsilon}\left( \nabla^{ver} \varphi(x,S^{u_\varepsilon}(x)), S^{u_\varepsilon}(x)X(x) \right) \cdot N_{u_\varepsilon}(x) - \varphi(x,S^{u_\varepsilon}(x)) H_{u_\varepsilon}(x) \cdot X(x) \, dV^{u_\varepsilon}. \]
We will pass $\varepsilon=\varepsilon_i \to 0$ in \eqref{E:ep-curvature-varifold} to deduce that $V=\lim_{i \to \infty} V^{\varepsilon_i}$ is in fact a curvature varifold with boundary. Moreover, we only present below the proof in the case $g_i=g$ and $\Omega_i=\Omega$ for all $i$ and leave the trivial modifications to the readers. In order to do this, we shall prove that all terms in \eqref{E:ep-curvature-varifold} can be suitably bounded as \textit{measure-function pairs} (see Appendix \ref{ap:measure-function}, or \cite{HutchinsonIUMJ, Tonegawa2005a}) and appeal to the compactness result of measure-function pairs as in Proposition \ref{prop:measure-function compactness}. With this goal in mind, we define a tensor-valued $\mathbf{B}_{V^{u_\varepsilon}} \in L^1_{loc}(G(U);V^{u_\varepsilon})$ with values in $T^*\widetilde{M} \otimes T^* \widetilde{M} \otimes T \widetilde{M}$ by
\[ \mathbf{B}_{V^{u_\varepsilon}}(X,Y)= B_{u_\varepsilon}(S(X),S(Y)) \]
and a vector-valued Radon measure $\partial V^{u_\varepsilon}$ on $G(U)$ with values in $T\widetilde{M}$ by (recall \eqref{E:mu-hat-partial})
\[ \partial V^{u_\varepsilon}= S(\nu(x)) \, d\hat{\mu}^\partial_{u_\varepsilon} \otimes \delta_{S^{u_\varepsilon}} \]
which clearly fulfil \eqref{E:formal-properties-B} and \eqref{E:formal-properties-partial-V}. By the well-known formula for the second fundamental form of a level set (see e.g. \cite[Lemma 3.3]{Hiesmayr2017}), 
		\begin{equation}
        \label{E:sff vs enhanced sff}
			|\mathbf{B}_{V^{u_{\varepsilon_i}}}|^2(x, S) \leq |B_{u_\varepsilon}|^2(x) \leq \frac{|\operatorname{Hess} u_\varepsilon|^2-\big|\nabla|\nabla u_\varepsilon|\big|^2}{|\nabla u_\varepsilon|^2}=|\mathcal{A}_{u_\varepsilon}|^2(x),
		\end{equation}
		where $\mathcal{A}_{u_\varepsilon}$ denotes the enhanced second fundamental form as defined in \eqref{E:enhanced-sff}.

In order to apply the compactness theorem (Proposition \ref{prop:measure-function compactness}), we need to show that $\mathbf{B}_{V^{u_\varepsilon}}$ is locally uniformly bounded in $L^2(B_r(x_0);V^{u_\varepsilon})$ independent of $\varepsilon$. Set (recall \eqref{E:cutoff}) $\psi(x)=\chi(\operatorname{dist}_g(x,x_0))$ in the $\varepsilon$-stability inequality \eqref{E:ep-stability}, we see that (recall that $\mu^\partial_{u_\varepsilon} \leq 2 \hat{\mu}^\partial_{u_\varepsilon}$ by definitions \eqref{E:mu-partial} and \eqref{E:mu-hat-partial})
\begin{equation}
\label{E:B^ep is L^2 bounded}
			\begin{aligned}
				\int_{G(B_{r/2}(x_0))} |\mathbf{B}_{V^{u_\varepsilon}}|^2(x, S) \, dV^{u_\varepsilon} & \leq \int_{B_{r/2}(x_0)} |\mathcal{A}_{u_\varepsilon}|^2\; d\|V^{u_\varepsilon}\|	\\
                & \leq \left(\frac{4}{r^2} + (n-1)k_0 \right) \|V^{u_\varepsilon}\|(B_r(x_0)) + 2 \kappa_0  \hat{\mu}^\partial_{u_\varepsilon}(B_r(x_0))\\
				                                                                                                                          
				                                                                                                                                                                                           
			\end{aligned} 
		\end{equation}
By Assumptions (A1) in \ref{As:stationary-AC}, we have a uniform bound on the first term of \eqref{E:B^ep is L^2 bounded} since 
\[ \|V^{u_\varepsilon}\|(B_r(x_0)) \leq \frac{2}{h_0}E_\varepsilon (u_\varepsilon) \leq \frac{2 E_0}{h_0}.\]
On the other hand, by \cite[Lemma 3.2]{LiPariseSarnataro2023} (with $\Lambda_0=0$ in our case), we also have a uniform bound on the second term of \eqref{E:B^ep is L^2 bounded}:
\begin{equation}
\label{E:mu-partial-bound}
\hat{\mu}^\partial_{u_\varepsilon}(B_r(x_0)) \leq C(n,k_0,\kappa_0,r) \frac{E_\varepsilon(u_\varepsilon)}{h_0} \leq C(n,k_0,\kappa_0,r) \frac{E_0}{h_0}.
\end{equation} 
Putting these back into \eqref{E:B^ep is L^2 bounded}, we have the following local uniform $L^2$-bounds:
\begin{equation}
    \label{E:B^ep is L^2 bounded-2}
\int_{G(B_{r/2}(x_0))} |\mathbf{B}_{V^{u_\varepsilon}}|^2(x, S) \, dV^{u_\varepsilon} \leq C(n,k_0,\kappa_0,r) \frac{E_0}{h_0}.
\end{equation} 

We consider now a sequence of positive numbers $\varepsilon_i\to 0$ and a sequence of solutions $\{u_{\varepsilon_i}\}_{i=1}^\infty$ to \eqref{E:Allen-Cahn} as in Theorem \ref{T:old-main}. By \eqref{E:B^ep is L^2 bounded-2}, we can apply Proposition \ref{prop:measure-function compactness} in Appendix \ref{ap:measure-function}\footnote{The result there is applicable since no metric structure is required (except the fact that $X=G(B_r(x_0))$ is a locally compact Hausdorff space) and hence is independent of the Riemannian metric.} to extract a subsequence such that 
\[ (\mathbf{B}_{V^{u_{\varepsilon_i}}},V^{u_{\varepsilon_i}}) \rightharpoonup (\mathbf{B}_V,V) \]
where $\mathbf{B}_V \in L^1_{loc}(G(B_r(x_0));V) $ is a tensor-valued map with values in $T^*\widetilde{M} \otimes T^* \widetilde{M} \otimes T \widetilde{M}$, and 
\begin{equation} 
\int  \mathbf{B}_{V^{u_{\varepsilon_i}}}(X,Y)\cdot Z \; dV^{u_{\varepsilon_i}} \to \int \mathbf{B}_V (X,Y)\cdot Z \; dV
\end{equation}
for any continuous vector fields $X,Y,Z$ on $G(B_r(x_0))$ with values in $T\widetilde{M}$. This clearly implies $\mathbf{B}_V$ satisfies \eqref{E:formal-properties-B}. On the other hand, the uniform bound \eqref{E:mu-partial-bound} implies that, after passing to a subsequence, 
\[ \partial V^{u_{\varepsilon_i}} \rightharpoonup \partial V=\textbf{n}_V \|\partial V\|\]
for some vector-valued Radon measure $\partial V$ on $G(B_r(x_0))$ with values in $T\widetilde{M}$. From the definition of $\partial V^{u_{\varepsilon_i}}$, it is clear that $\partial V$ satisfies \eqref{E:formal-properties-partial-V}. Passing $\varepsilon_i \to 0$ in \eqref{E:ep-curvature-varifold}, using that $\xi_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i}) \to 0$ in $L^1(\Omega \cap U;d\mathrm{vol}_g)$ by Theorem \ref{T:old-main} (1), we obtain 
\[ \int \varphi(x,S) X(x) \cdot \textbf{n}_V(x,S) \; d\|\partial V\|=\int S \left(\nabla^{hor} \varphi (x,S) \right) \cdot X(x) + \varphi(x,S) \operatorname{div}_{S} X(x) \; dV \] 
\begin{equation}
\label{E:V-curvature-varifold}
    + \int \mathbf{B}_V \left( \nabla^{ver} \varphi(x,S), S(X(x)) \right) \cdot N_S - \varphi(x,S) \mathbf{H}_V(x,S) \cdot X(x) \; dV
\end{equation}
where $\mathbf{H}_V=\operatorname{Tr} \mathbf{B}_V \in L^1_{loc}(G(B_r(x_0));V)$ with values in $T\widetilde{M}$. This shows that $V$ is a curvature varifold with boundary in any geodesic ball $B_r(x_0)$ according to Definition \ref{D:curvature-varifolds}. Using a locally finite open covering of $U$ by such balls, (1) follows by Remark \ref{R:curvature-varifold-local}.

We now proceed to prove the stability type inequality \eqref{E:stability-inequality-boundary} in (2). First of all, since $\partial V^{u_{\varepsilon_i}} \rightharpoonup \partial V$, combined with the fact that $V$ is a free boundary varifold with $\operatorname{spt}\|V\| \subset \overline{\Omega} \cap U$ by Theorem \ref{T:old-main} (4) and using Proposition \ref{P:free-boundary-characterization} together with \eqref{E:relation-to-first-variation}, we have
\begin{equation}
\label{E:partial-V-convergence}
\nu \,  \hat{\mu}^\partial_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}=\pi_*(\partial V^{u_{\varepsilon_i}}) \rightharpoonup \pi_*(\partial V)=\nu  \|\delta V\|_{sing}.
\end{equation}
Therefore, $\hat{\mu}^\partial_{u_{\varepsilon_i}} \rightharpoonup \|\delta V\|_{sing}$. By the lower semi-continuity property \eqref{E:lower-semicontinuity-measure-function} in Proposition \ref{prop:measure-function compactness}, \eqref{E:sff vs enhanced sff} and the $\varepsilon$-stability inequality \eqref{E:ep-stability}, since $V \in \mathbf{IV}_{n-1}(U)$ by Theorem \ref{T:old-main} (3), for any $\psi\in C^1(U)$ we have (recall again that $\mu^\partial_{u_\varepsilon} \leq 2\hat{\mu}^\partial_{u_\varepsilon}$)
\[			\int |\mathbf{B}_V(x,T_xV)|^2\psi^2(x)\;d\|V\|(x)  \leq \liminf_{i \to\infty} \int |B_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}(x)|^2 \psi^2(x) \; d \mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}(x) \]
\[ \leq \int |\nabla \psi|^2 \; d\|V\| + (n-1)k_0 \int \psi^2 \; d\|V\| + 2 \kappa_0 \int \psi^2 \; d\|\delta V\|_{sing} \]
which is the desired inequality \eqref{E:stability-inequality-boundary}.

Finally, we prove assertion (3). Let $\varepsilon=\varepsilon_i \to 0$ in \eqref{E:diffused-V-first-variation}, by the continuity of first variation with respect to varifold convergence, together with Theorem \ref{T:old-main} (1) and \eqref{E:partial-V-convergence}, we obtain for any $X \in \mathfrak{X}_c(U)$, 
\[ \delta V(X)= \lim_{i \to \infty} \delta V^{u_{\varepsilon_i}}(X)= \int X \cdot \nu \; d \|\delta V\|_{sing}.\]
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- remark-28: body_md -->
Let $W \subset\subset U \setminus \mathrm{sing}(V)$ be an open set such that $W \cap \mathrm{reg}(V) \neq \emptyset$, and let $\phi\in C^1_c(W)$. In this case, we can get a more precise stability inequality as follows. First, note that, by \eqref{E:varifold-def}, 
	\begin{equation}
		\int_U \operatorname{Ric}(x) (N_{u_{\varepsilon_i}(x)}, N_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}(x)) \phi^2(x) \, d\|V^{u_{\varepsilon_i}}\|(x)=\int_{G(U)} \operatorname{Ric}(x)(N_S, N_S) \phi^2 (x)\, d V^{u_{\varepsilon_i}}(x,S).
	\end{equation}
	                                                                                                                                                                                                                         
	Letting $\varepsilon_i \to 0$ and denoting $\Sigma=\operatorname{reg} V$, we have
    \[ \lim_{i \to \infty} \int_U \operatorname{Ric}(x) (N_{u_{\varepsilon_i}(x)}, N_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}(x)) \phi^2(x) \, d\|V^{u_{\varepsilon_i}}\|(x)= \int_U \operatorname{Ric} (N_\Sigma,N_\Sigma) \phi^2\, d\|V\|. \]
	                                                                                                                                                                    
	              
	                                                                                                         
	                                               
	            
	                                                                                     
	Therefore, when $\phi\in C^1_c(W)$ with $W \cap \mathrm{reg}(V) \neq \emptyset$, the stability inequality \eqref{E:stability-inequality-boundary} becomes
	\begin{equation}
		\int_U \left(|B_\Sigma|^2+\operatorname{Ric} (N_\Sigma, N_\Sigma) \right)\phi^2 \, d\|V\| \leq \int_U |\nabla \phi|^2 \, d\|V\| + 2 \kappa_0 \int_U \phi^2 \, d\|\delta V\|_{sing}.
	\end{equation}
    However, it is unclear whether one can recover the boundary term as in the classical case even when $\phi$ is supported inside $\operatorname{reg} V$ because we do not know the convergence of the Radon measures $\mu^\partial_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}$ instead of $\hat{\mu}^\partial_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}$.
<!-- end remark-28 -->
<!-- remark-29: body_md -->
By the trace inequality in Lemma \ref{L:trace-inequality}, \eqref{E:stability-inequality-boundary} implies for any $\delta>0$
    \begin{equation}
    \label{E:stability-inequality-boundary-removed}
    \int |\mathbf{B}_V|^2\psi^2 \, d\|V\| \leq (1+\delta)\int |\nabla\psi|^2 \, d\|V\| + C(n,i_0,k_0,\kappa_0,\delta) \int \psi^2 \, d\|V\|
    \end{equation}
    which is expressed entirely in interior terms resembling the concept of $\delta$-stability as studied in \cite{Hong-Li-Wang2024}, for example.
<!-- end remark-29 -->
<!-- definition-6: body_md -->
\label{D:interior-classical-cone}
    Let $\mathbf{C} \in \mathbf{IV}_{n-1}(\mathbb{R}^n)$ be a stationary integral varifold cone (i.e. $(\eta_{0,r})_\sharp \mathbf{C}=\mathbf{C}$ for all $r>0$). We say that $\mathbf{C}$ is an \emph{interior classical tangent cone} if 
    \[ \operatorname{spt} \|\mathbf{C}\| = \bigcup_{j=1}^N P_j \]
    for some $N \geq 3$, and each $P_j \subset \mathbb{R}^n$ is a distinct half-hyperplane meeting along a common $(n-2)$-dimensional subspace.
<!-- end definition-6 -->
<!-- remark-30: body_md -->
By the stationarity and integrality of $\mathbf{C}$, in fact we have as varifolds
    \[ \mathbf{C}=\sum_{j=1}^N \Theta_j |P_j| \quad \text{ for some constants $\Theta_j \in \mathbb{N}$.} \]
    Moreover, if we denote $\eta_j$ to be the outward unit co-normal of $\partial P_j$ with respect to the half-hyperplane $P_j$, then stationarity implies the balancing condition
    \[ \sum_{j=1}^N \Theta_j \eta_j=0\]
    is satisfied along the spine $S(\mathbf{C})=\cap_{j=1}^N P_j$ (recall Definition \ref{D:spine}).
<!-- end remark-30 -->
<!-- definition-7: body_md -->
\label{D:boundary-classical-cone}
    Let $\mathbf{C} \in \mathbf{IV}_{n-1}(\mathbb{R}^n)$ be a free boundary stationary integral varifold cone (i.e. $(\eta_{0,r})_\sharp \mathbf{C}=\mathbf{C}$ for all $r>0$) with free boundary on $\partial \mathbb{R}^n_+$ and $\operatorname{spt} \|\mathbf{C}\| \subset \mathbb{R}^n_+$. We say that $\mathbf{C}$ is a \emph{boundary classical tangent cone} if 
    \[ \operatorname{spt} \|\mathbf{C}\| = \bigcup_{j=1}^N P_j \cap \mathbb{R}^n_+ \]
    for some $N \geq 2$, and each $P_j \subset \mathbb{R}^n$ is a distinct half-hyperplane meeting along a common $(n-2)$-dimensional subspace. We further divide the case according to the dichotomy \eqref{E:dichotomy-cones} on the spine of the doubled cone $\overline{\mathbf{C}}$ (as defined in Lemma \ref{L:reflection stationary}). We say that $\mathbf{C}$ is of
    \begin{itemize}
        \item \emph{Type I}: if $S(\overline{\mathbf{C}}) \subset \partial \mathbb{R}^n_+$, $N \geq 2$, and each $P_j \subset \mathbb{R}^n_+$ but $\operatorname{spt} \|\mathbf{C}\| \neq \partial \mathbb{R}^n_+$;
        \item \emph{Type II}: if $S(\overline{\mathbf{C}}) \perp \partial \mathbb{R}^n_+$ and $N \geq 3$.
    \end{itemize}
<!-- end definition-7 -->
<!-- remark-31: body_md -->
By the free boundary stationarity and integrality of $\mathbf{C}$, in fact we have as varifolds
    \[ \mathbf{C}=\sum_{j=1}^N \Theta_j |P_j| \quad \text{ for some constants $\Theta_j \in \mathbb{N}$} \]
    if $\mathbf{C}$ is of type I (c.f. Example \ref{Ex:boundary-classical-cones}). Moreover, if we denote $\eta_j$ to be the outward unit co-normal of $\partial P_j$ with respect to the half-hyperplane $P_j$, then free boundary stationarity implies the free boundary balancing condition
    \[ \sum_{j=1}^N \Theta_j \eta_j \perp \partial \mathbb{R}^n_+\]
    is satisfied along the spine $S(\overline{\mathbf{C}})=\cap_{j=1}^N P_j \subset \partial \mathbb{R}^n_+$. If $\mathbf{C}$ is of type II, then the doubled cone $\overline{\mathbf{C}}$ is in fact an interior classical tangent cone with $S(\overline{\mathbf{C}}) \perp \partial \mathbb{R}^n_+$.
<!-- end remark-31 -->
<!-- theorem-6: body_md -->
\label{T:exclude-classical-cones}
Let $\{\varepsilon_i\}_{i=1}^\infty$ be a sequence of positive real numbers such that $\varepsilon_i\to 0$, and let $\{u_{\varepsilon_i}\}_{i=1}^\infty$ be a sequence of solutions to \eqref{E:Allen-Cahn} satisfying (A1)-(A2) in Assumptions \ref{As:stationary-AC}. Assume, furthermore, that each $u_{\varepsilon_i}$ is stable, i.e. (A3) in Assumption \ref{As:stable-AC} is satisfied. Then, for any limit-interface $V \in \mathbf{IV}_{n-1}(U)$ obtained from Theorem \ref{T:old-main},
\[ \text{there exists a Borel set $\mathcal{Z} \subset \operatorname{spt} \|V\|$ with $\mathcal{H}^{n-2}(\mathcal{Z})=0$} \]
such that for any $x \in \operatorname{spt}\|V\| \setminus \mathcal{Z}$, there does not exist $\mathbf{C} \in \operatorname{VarTan}(V,x)$ which is an interior classical tangent cone in $T_x \widetilde{M}$ when $x \in \Omega$; or a boundary classical tangent cone in $T^+_x \widetilde{M}$ when $x \in \partial \Omega$.
<!-- end theorem-6 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
Our proof follows the main strategy of \cite[Proposition 3.2]{Tonegawa2012} which treats the interior case in the Euclidean setting. First of all, the $\varepsilon$-stability inequality \eqref{E:ep-stability} implies that after passing to a subsequence, we have
\[ |\mathcal{A}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}|^2 \; d\mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}} \rightharpoonup \mu\]
as Radon measures on $U$. Now, we define 
\begin{equation}
    \mathcal{Z}:=\left\{ x \in \operatorname{spt}\|V\| \; : \; \limsup_{r \to 0} \frac{\mu(B_r(x))}{r^{n-3}} >0  \right\},
\end{equation}
which is a Borel set since $\mu$ is a Radon measure on $U$. Moreover, we have $\mathcal{H}^{n-3+\gamma}(\mathcal{Z})=0$ for every $\gamma>0$ (c.f. \cite[Remark 3.1 and Theorem 3.2]{Simon1983}).

We now show that such $\mathcal{Z}$ satisfies the assertion in the theorem by a contradiction argument as in \cite{Tonegawa2012}. Suppose on the contrary that there exists $x \in \operatorname{spt}\|V\| \setminus \mathcal{Z}$ such that some tangent cone of $V$ at $x$ is a classical tangent cone. We will focus on the case $x \in \partial \Omega$ is a boundary point (as the interior case is similar and has been treated in the Euclidean setting in \cite{Tonegawa2012}). Therefore, we would obtain a tangent cone $\mathbf{C} \in \operatorname{VarTan}(V,x)$ at $x \in \partial \Omega$ which is a boundary classical tangent cone in $T^+_x \widetilde{M}$. There are two cases to consider according to whether $\mathbf{C}$ is of type I or type II as described in Definition \ref{D:boundary-classical-cone}.

We first look at the case where $\mathbf{C}$ is of type I, i.e. 
\[ T_x \partial \Omega \neq \operatorname{spt} \|\mathbf{C}\| = \bigcup_{j=1}^N P_j  \]
for some $N \geq 2$, and each $P_j \subset T^+_x \widetilde{M}$ is a distinct half-hyperplane meeting along the $(n-2)$-dimensional spine $S(\overline{\mathbf{C}}) \subset T_x \partial \Omega$. After a rotation, we may assume that $S(\overline{\mathbf{C}})=\{0\} \times \mathbb{R}^{n-2} \subset \mathbb{R}^2_+ \times \mathbb{R}^{n-2}$ and the half-hyperplanes $P_j$ can be expressed as
\[ P_j=\{t \textbf{p}_j \; : \; t \geq 0\} \times \mathbb{R}^{n-2}\]
for some distinct vectors $\textbf{p}_j \in \mathbb{R}^2_+$ with $|\textbf{p}_j|=1/2$.

\vspace{1ex}

\textit{Step 1: Passing the analysis back to the $\varepsilon$-level.}

\vspace{1ex}

Since $\mathbf{C} \in \operatorname{VarTan}(V,x)$ is a tangent cone at $x$, we have $\lim_{i \to \infty} (\eta_{0,r_i})_\sharp (\exp_x^{-1})_\sharp V=\mathbf{C}$ for some $r_i \to 0$. On the other hand, $V=\lim_{i \to \infty} V^{u_{\varepsilon_i}}$ for some $\varepsilon_i \to 0$, where each $u_{\varepsilon_i}$ is a stable solution to \eqref{E:Allen-Cahn} with $\varepsilon=\varepsilon_i$ satisfying (A1)-(A2) in Assumptions \ref{As:stationary-AC}. After passing to a subsequence, we can assume without loss of generality that
\begin{equation}
\label{E:proof-exclude-classical-cone-1}
\lim_{i \to \infty} (\eta_{0,r_i})_\sharp (\exp_x^{-1})_\sharp V^{u_{\varepsilon_i}}=\mathbf{C} \quad \text{ and } \quad \lim_{i \to \infty} \frac{\varepsilon_i}{r_i}=0. 
\end{equation}
Setting 
\[ \tilde{\varepsilon}_i:=\frac{\varepsilon_i}{r_i} \to 0 \quad \text{ and } \quad \tilde{u}_{\tilde{\varepsilon}_i}:= u_{\varepsilon_i} \circ \exp_x \circ  \eta_{0,r_i^{-1}}.\]
If we let $\Phi_{x,r_i}:=\exp_x \circ  \eta_{0,r_i^{-1}} :B^\delta_{i_0/2}(0) \to B^g_{r_ii_0/2}(x)$ which is a $C^2$ diffeomorphism taking $\Omega_i \cap B^\delta_R(0)$ to $\Omega \cap B^g_{Rr_i}(x)$ for any fixed $R < \min\{\frac{i_0}{2}, \frac{1}{2\kappa_0} \}$ where $\Omega_i \subset T_x\widetilde{M}$ are $C^2$ domains satisfying
\begin{equation}
    \label{E:domain-convergence}
    \Omega_i \cap B^\delta_R(0) \text{ converges locally in $C^2$ to } T^+_x \widetilde{M} \cap B^\delta_R(0),
\end{equation}
Therefore, $\tilde{u}_{\tilde{\varepsilon}_i} \in C^2(\overline{\Omega}_i \cap B^\delta_R(0))$ are stable solutions to the homogeneous Allen-Cahn equation
\begin{equation}
\label{E:u-tilde-AC}
-\tilde{\varepsilon}_i^2 \Delta_{\tilde{g}_i} \tilde{u}_{\tilde{\varepsilon}_i} +W'(\tilde{u}_{\varepsilon_i})=0 \qquad \text{in $\Omega_i \cap B^\delta_R(0)$} 
\end{equation}
where $\tilde{g}_i:= r_i^{-2}\Phi^*_{x,r_i} g$ is a Riemannian metric on $B^\delta_R(0)$, together with the homogeneous Neumann boundary condition
\[ \frac{\partial \tilde{u}_{\tilde{\varepsilon}_i}}{\partial \tilde{\nu}_i}=0 \qquad \text{ along $\partial \Omega_i \cap B^\delta_R(0)$}\]
where $\tilde{\nu}_i$ is the outward unit normal of $\partial \Omega_i$ with respect to the metric $\tilde{g}_i$. Note that the Riemannian metrics $\tilde{g}_i$ converge uniformly on $B^\delta_R(0)$ in $C^2$ to the flat metric $\delta$ on $T_x \widetilde{M}$ and $\Omega_i$ converges in $C^2$-sense to $T_x^+\widetilde{M}$ by \eqref{E:domain-convergence}. 

We now check that $\tilde{u}_{\tilde{\varepsilon}_i}$ satisfies uniform bounds (A1)-(A2) with $U=B^\delta_R(0) \subset T_x \widetilde{M}$ with respect to the metric $\tilde{g}_i$ in Assumptions \ref{As:stationary-AC}. It is clear from the definition that (A2) is satisfied by $\tilde{u}_{\tilde{\varepsilon}_i}$ with the same $C_0$. On the other hand, the uniform energy bound (A1), with a different $\tilde{E}_0$, can be seen from the almost-monotonicity formula applied to $u_{\varepsilon_i}$ (c.f. \cite[Proposition 4.15]{LiPariseSarnataro2023}). Therefore, Theorem \ref{T:old-main} can be applied to the varifolds $V^{\tilde{u}_{\tilde{\varepsilon}_i}} \in \mathbf{V}_{n-1}(B^\delta_R(0))$ defined by
\begin{equation}
\label{E:varifold-def-tilde}
	V^{\tilde{u}_{\tilde{\varepsilon}_i}}(\phi) := \int_{\{y \in \Omega_i \cap B^\delta_R(0): |\nabla^{\tilde{g}_i} \tilde{u}_{\tilde{\varepsilon}_i}(y)|\neq 0\}}\phi\left(y, S^{\tilde{u}_{\tilde{\varepsilon}_i}}(y)\right)\ d\mu_{\tilde{u}_{\tilde{\varepsilon}_i}}(y) \qquad \forall \phi\in C_c(G(B^\delta_R(0))).
\end{equation}
It is straightforward from \eqref{E:proof-exclude-classical-cone-1} and the definitions that
\begin{equation}
    \label{E:V-tilde-convergence}
 V^{\tilde{u}_{\tilde{\varepsilon}_i}} \to \mathbf{C} \quad \text{as varifolds in $\mathbf{V}_{n-1}(B^\delta_R(0))$}.
\end{equation}
Recall that $x \in \operatorname{spt} \|V\| \setminus \mathcal{Z}$, after passing to a subsequence of $\{\varepsilon_i\}$, we can further assume that
\[ \lim_{i\to \infty} \int_{B^\delta_2(0)} |\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\tilde{u}_{\tilde{\varepsilon}_i}}|^2 \; d\tilde{\mu}_{\tilde{u}_{\tilde{\varepsilon}_i}}=  \lim_{i \to \infty} \frac{1}{(2r_i)^{n-3}} \int_{B^g_{2r_i}(x)} |\mathcal{A}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}|^2 d\mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}=0 \]
where the first integral is computed with respect to the metric $\tilde{g}_i$. 

Therefore, after suitably passing to subsequences, we can reduce our problem to the following situation (we are omitting the tildes for simplicity): let $U=B_2 \subset \mathbb{R}^n$ denote the Euclidean ball of radius $2$ centred at the origin, there exists a sequence of positive real numbers $\{\varepsilon_i\}_{i=1}^\infty$ such that
\[ \lim_{i \to \infty} \varepsilon_i=0\]
and a sequence $\{u_{\varepsilon_i}\}_{i=1}^\infty$ of $C^2$ solutions to the homogeneous Allen-Cahn equation
\[ -\varepsilon_i^2 \Delta_{g_i}u_{\varepsilon_i} +W'(u_{\varepsilon_i})=0 \qquad \text{ in $\Omega_i \cap B_2$}\]
with homogeneous Neumann boundary condition
\[ \frac{\partial u_{\varepsilon_i}}{\partial \nu_i} = 0 \qquad \text{ along $\partial \Omega_i \cap B_2$}\]
where $\{g_i\}_{i=1}^\infty$ is a sequence of $C^2$ Riemannian metrics on $B_2$ and $\{\Omega_i\}_{i=1}^\infty$ is a sequence of Euclidean $C^2$ domains in $\mathbb{R}^n$ with outward unit normal $\nu_i$ (with respect to $g_i$) such that
\begin{equation}
    \label{E:ambient-convergence}
    \lim_{i \to \infty} g_i = \delta \qquad \text{ and } \qquad \lim_{i \to \infty} \Omega_i = \mathbb{R}^n_+
\end{equation}
in the $C^2$-sense. Moreover, $\{u_{\varepsilon_i}\}_{i=1}^\infty$ satisfies the uniform bounds (A1)-(A2) in Assumptions \ref{As:stationary-AC}; $\{g_i\}_{i=1}^\infty$ and are stable in the sense of \eqref{E:def-stability} in (A3), Assumptions \ref{As:stable-AC}. Moreover, we have in addition
\begin{equation}
    \label{E:blow-up-argument}
    \lim_{i \to \infty} V^{u_{\varepsilon_i}}=\mathbf{C} \qquad \text{ and } \qquad \lim_{i\to \infty} \int_{B_2} |\mathcal{A}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}|^2 \; d\mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}=0.
\end{equation}
Moreover, we set up our coordinates $(y,z) \in  \mathbb{R}^2_+ \times \mathbb{R}^{n-2}$ so that
\[ S(\overline{\mathbf{C}})=\{(0,z) \; : \; z \in \mathbb{R}^{n-2}\}=\{0\} \times \mathbb{R}^{n-2}.\]

                 
                                                                                           
               
                                                        


                                  
                 
                     
                                                                                                                                                                                                    
               
                                                                                                                                                
                                                                                                                                
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 
                                                                                                                                                                     
                                                                                                                                                                                                


\vspace{1ex}

\textit{Step 2: Show that, in the limit sense as $\varepsilon_i \to 0$, for $\mathcal{L}^{n-2}$-a.e. point on the spine $S(\overline{\mathbf{C}})$, the $y$-cross sections of all the intermediate level sets of $u_{\varepsilon_i}$ are uniformly regular.}

\vspace{1ex}

More precisely, we claim that there exists some $c=c(W)>0$ such that if we denote the set of \emph{irregular cross sections} by
\[ \mathcal{I}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}:=\left\{ z \in \mathbb{R}^{n-2} \; : \; 
\begin{array}{l}
            \textrm{$|z| < 1$ and for any $r>0$, there exists some $z' \in B_r(z)$ such that }
            \\
            \textrm{$| \nabla^{g_i} u_{\varepsilon_i}(y',z')|_{g_i} < \frac{c}{\varepsilon_i}$ for some $|y'|<1$ and $|u_{\varepsilon_i}(y',z')| \leq \frac{1}{2}$}
        \end{array} \right\}, \]
then 
\begin{equation}
\label{E:step-2-proof}
\lim_{i \to \infty} \mathcal{L}^{n-2}(\mathcal{I}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}})=0.
\end{equation}

\begin{remark}
    Note that by standard elliptic estimates (c.f. \cite[Lemma 4.7]{LiPariseSarnataro2023}), we have the uniform gradient estimate $\varepsilon_i | \nabla^{g_i} u_{\varepsilon_i}(y',z')|_{g_i} \leq C$ for some large enough $C>0$. Therefore, \eqref{E:step-2-proof} amounts to showing that in a limiting measure-theoretic sense, inside the transition layer $\{|u_{\varepsilon_i}|\leq \frac{1}{2}\}$, the gradient of $u_{\varepsilon_i}$ is indeed of order $\frac{1}{\varepsilon_i}$.
\end{remark}

The proof first involves an estimate of the left hand side of \eqref{E:step-2-proof} by a combination of covering arguments, standard elliptic estimates and the two-dimensional Gagliardo–Nirenberg–Sobolev inequality. Then, by the vanishing of the discrepancy function in the $L^1$-limit (Theorem \ref{T:old-main}(1)) and the simplifying assumption \eqref{E:blow-up-argument} from Step 1, we can obtain \eqref{E:step-2-proof} verbatim from the proof of \cite[Lemma 4.1]{Tonegawa2012}.


\vspace{1ex}

\textit{Step 3: Show that, in the limit sense as $\varepsilon_i \to 0$, for $\mathcal{L}^{n-2}$-a.e. point on the spine $S(\overline{\mathbf{C}})$, we have at a fixed distance from the spine the cross sections of all the intermediate level sets of $u_{\varepsilon}$ concentrate near every $P_j$.}

\vspace{1ex}

Recall that 
\[\operatorname{spt} \|\mathbf{C}\| = \bigcup_{j=1}^N \{t \textbf{p}_j \; : \; t \geq 0\} \times \mathbb{R}^{n-2}\]
for some $N \geq 2$ and distinct vectors $\textbf{p}_j \in \mathbb{R}^2_+$ with $|\textbf{p}_j|=1/2$.  Fix $\sigma \in (0,\frac{1}{4})$ such that $\sigma < \frac{1}{2}\min_{j \neq k}|\textbf{p}_j - \textbf{p}_k|$ so that the balls $B_{2\sigma}(\mathbf{p}_j)$ are mutually disjoint. Define the \emph{non-concentration set} along the spine by
\[ \mathcal{N}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}:=\left\{ z \in \mathbb{R}^{n-2} \; : \; 
\begin{array}{l}
            \textrm{$|z| < 1$, and there exists some $t' \in [-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]$ and $j'=1,\cdots,N$ }
            \\
            \textrm{such that $u_{\varepsilon_i}(y,z) \neq t'$ for all $y$ with $|y-\textbf{p}_{j'}|<\sigma$}
        \end{array} \right\}, \]
then we claim that
\begin{equation}
\label{E:step-3-proof}
\lim_{i \to \infty} \mathcal{L}^{n-2}(\mathcal{N}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}})=0.
\end{equation}

To prove \eqref{E:step-3-proof}, it suffices to establish $ \lim_{i \to \infty} \mathcal{L}^{n-2}(\mathcal{N}^j_{u_{\varepsilon_i}})=0$ for each $j=1,\cdots,N$, where 
\[ \mathcal{N}^j_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}:=\left\{ z \in \mathbb{R}^{n-2} \; : \; 
\begin{array}{l}
            \textrm{$|z| < 1$, and there exists some $t' \in [-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]$ }
            \\
            \textrm{such that $u_{\varepsilon_i}(y,z) \neq t'$ for all $y$ with $|y-\textbf{p}_j|<\sigma$}
        \end{array} \right\}. \]

For notational simplicity, we will fix $j$ and write the vector $\textbf{p}=\textbf{p}_j \in \mathbb{R}^2_+$ and the $(n-1)$-dimensional half-hyperplane $P=P_j$. There are two cases to consider: either $\textbf{p} \in \mathbb{R}^2_+ \setminus \partial \mathbb{R}^2_+$ or $\textbf{p} \in \partial \mathbb{R}^2_+$. We will focus only on the latter case as the former case is similar and resembles the interior theory treated in \cite[Lemma 4.2]{Tonegawa2012}. From now on, we take
\[\textbf{p}=\left( \frac{1}{2},0 \right) \in \mathbb{R}^2_+ \qquad \text{ and } \qquad P=\{(y_1,0,z)\; : \; y_1 \geq 0, z \in \mathbb{R}^{n-2}\}  \subset \mathbb{R}^n_+,\]
and we will denote, for any $z' \in \mathbb{R}^{n-2}$, the half-cylinder of size $\frac{\sigma}{2}$ centred at $(\textbf{p},z')$ by
\[ C_{\frac{\sigma}{2}} (\textbf{p},z'):=\left\{(y_1,y_2,z) \; : \; |y_1-\frac{1}{2}|^2+|z-z'|^2 < \left( \frac{\sigma}{2} \right)^2, \, 0 \leq y_2 < \frac{\sigma}{2} \right\} \subset \mathbb{R}^n_+. \]
Moreover, we define the set of exceptional points on $P$ centred at $(\textbf{p},z')$ as follows:
\begin{equation}
    \label{E:exceptional-set}
    S_i(\textbf{p},z'):=\left\{(y_1,0,z) \in P \cap C_{\frac{\sigma}{2}} (\textbf{p},z') \; : \; \exists t' \in \left[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\right] \text{ s.t. } u_{\varepsilon_i}(y_1,y_2,z) \neq t' \; \forall y_2 \in \left[0,\frac{\sigma}{2}\right) \right\}.
\end{equation}

The proof of \eqref{E:step-3-proof} is a contradiction argument following \cite[Lemma 4.2]{Tonegawa2012}, utilizing the results in our previous work \cite[Section 6]{LiPariseSarnataro2023}. Suppose, on the contrary that \eqref{E:step-3-proof} does not hold, then there exists some $z' \in \mathbb{R}^{n-2}$ with $|z'|<1$ such that
\[ \limsup_{i \to \infty} \mathcal{L}^{n-2}(B_{\frac{\sigma}{4}}(z') \cap \mathcal{N}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}})>0.\]
For any $z'' \in B_{\frac{\sigma}{4}}(z') \cap \mathcal{N}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}$, by the definition of $\mathcal{N}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}$ and $S_i(\textbf{p},z')$, we must have 
\[ (y_1,0,z'') \in S_i(\textbf{p},z') \quad \text{ provided that } \left|y_1-\frac{1}{2}\right|<\frac{\sigma}{4}.\]
Therefore, we have
\[\mathcal{L}^{n-1}(S_i(\textbf{p},z')) \geq \frac{\sigma}{2} \mathcal{L}^{n-2}(B_{\frac{\sigma}{4}}(z') \cap \mathcal{N}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}})\]
and thus $\limsup_{i \to \infty} \mathcal{L}^{n-1}(S_i(\textbf{p},z')) >0$ by the choice of $z'$. This will immediately lead to a contradiction once we show that
\begin{equation}
\label{E:step-3-proof-main-estimate}
\lim_{i \to \infty} \mathcal{L}^{n-1}(S_i(\textbf{p},z')) =0 \quad \text{ for any $|z'|<1$.}
\end{equation}

We now prove \eqref{E:step-3-proof-main-estimate}. Fix any $|z'|<1$. By Theorem \ref{T:old-main}(2), we can assume that 
\begin{equation}
    \label{E:uniform-converge-to-one}
\text{$u_{\varepsilon_i} \to 1$ locally uniformly on $B_\sigma(\textbf{p},z') \setminus P$.} 
\end{equation}
Take any $(y_1,0,z) \in S_i(\textbf{p},z')$, by definition and \eqref{E:uniform-converge-to-one}, we have for any given $b \in (0,\frac{1}{2})$ one of the following scenarios:
\begin{itemize}
    \item Scenario I: $u_{\varepsilon_i}(y_1,y_2,z) > 1-b$ for all $0 \leq y_2 < \frac{\sigma}{2}$;
    \item Scenario II: $u_{\varepsilon_i}(y_1,y_2,z) \leq 1-b$ for some $0 \leq y_2 < \frac{\sigma}{2}$, in which case we must have
    \begin{equation}
        \label{E:scenario-II-consequence}
    -\frac{1}{2} \leq \inf_{y_2 \in [0,\frac{\sigma}{2})} u_{\varepsilon_i}(y_1,y_2,z) \leq 1-b. 
    \end{equation} 
\end{itemize}
We will estimate the $\mathcal{L}^{n-1}$-measure of those $(y_1,0,z) \in S_i(\textbf{p},z')$ belonging to each of the two scenarios. For our purpose, we denote
\[ S^I_i(\textbf{p},z';b):=\left\{ (y_1,0,z) \in S_i(\textbf{p},z') \; : \; u_{\varepsilon_i}(y_1,y_2,z) > 1-b \text{ for all } 0 \leq y_2 < \frac{\sigma}{2} \right\}, \]
\[ S^{II}_i(\textbf{p},z';b):=\left\{ (y_1,0,z) \in S_i(\textbf{p},z') \; : \; u_{\varepsilon_i}(y_1,y_2,z) \leq 1-b \text{ for some } 0 \leq y_2 < \frac{\sigma}{2} \right\}, \]
where $b \in (0,\frac{1}{2})$ is a parameter to be chosen small enough later. Clearly 
\begin{equation}
    \label{E:decompose-S_i}
S_i(\textbf{p},z')=S^I_i(\textbf{p},z';b) \cup S^{II}_i(\textbf{p},z';b) \qquad \text{for any $b \in (0,\frac{1}{2})$}.
\end{equation}
To estimate $S^I_i(\textbf{p},z';b)$ and $S^{II}_i(\textbf{p},z';b)$, one needs to analyse points around which the level sets of $u_{\varepsilon_i}$ have relatively small (or large) scale-invariant ``tilt-excess''. For our convenience, we introduce the following notation (recall \eqref{E:energy-density-discrepancy}, \eqref{E:mu} and \eqref{E:u-normal} all taken with respect to the metric $g_i$ and domain $\Omega_i$): for any $x \in C_{\frac{\sigma}{2}} (\textbf{p},z')$ and $r \in (0,1)$, we denote the scale-invariant \emph{tilt-excess} of $u_{\varepsilon_i}$ inside the ball $B_r(x)$ with respect to the half-hyperplane $P$ by the non-negative quantity
\begin{equation}
    \label{E:excess}
    \widehat{E}(u_{\varepsilon_i};B_r(x)):= \frac{1}{r^{n-1}} \int_{B_r(x) \cap \Omega_i} |\xi_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i})| \; dv_{g_i} +  \big(1-g_i(N_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}, \nabla^{g_i} d_{g_i}^P)^2 \big) \; d\mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}},
\end{equation}
where $d_{g_i}^P$ is the signed distance function from $P$ with respect to the metric $g_i$. 

We now fix some parameters using the key propositions in \cite[Section 6]{LiPariseSarnataro2023}. For any given $s>0$, by \cite[Proposition 6.3]{LiPariseSarnataro2023}, we can choose $b \in (0,\frac{1}{2})$ sufficiently small such that
\[ (*) \quad \int_{B_2 \cap \{|u_{\varepsilon_i}| \geq 1-b\}} \frac{W(u_{\varepsilon_i})}{\varepsilon_i} \; dv_{g_i} \leq s \qquad \text{for all sufficiently large $i$}. \]
For any such $s$ and $b$, by \cite[Proposition 6.1]{LiPariseSarnataro2023} we can fix $\eta_1 \in (0,1)$ and $L>1$ such that\footnote{Recall that $g_i \to \delta$ and $\xi_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i}) \leq \varepsilon_i^{-4/5}$ by Theorem \ref{T:old-main}. Therefore, (ii) of \cite[Proposition 6.1]{LiPariseSarnataro2023} are satisfied for all sufficiently large $i$. Note that there is a typo in (ii) of \cite[Proposition 6.1]{LiPariseSarnataro2023}, $|\xi_{\tilde{\varepsilon}}|$ should be replaced by $\xi_{\tilde{\varepsilon}}$.}
\[ \begin{array}{l}
\text{(**) For $x=(y_1,y_2,z) \in C_{\frac{\sigma}{2}} (\textbf{p},z')$ with $|u_{\varepsilon_i}(x)| \leq 1-b$ and $\widehat{E}(u_{\varepsilon_i};B_{4 \varepsilon_i L}(x))\leq \eta_1$, and for all}\\
\text{sufficiently large $i$, we have $y_2>3\varepsilon_iL$ and $u_{\varepsilon_i}(y_1,y,z) \neq u_{\varepsilon_i}(x)$ for all $0<|y-y_2|<3 \varepsilon_i L$.}\\
\text{Moreover, inside $B_{\varepsilon_i L}(x)$, $u_{\varepsilon_i}$ is $C^1$-close to the unique one-dimensional finite-energy heteroclinic}\\
\text{solution $q_{\varepsilon_i}(y_2)$ satisfying $q_{\varepsilon_i}(y_2)=u_{\varepsilon_i}(x)$ and}
\end{array}
\]
\begin{equation} 
\label{E:**}
1-s \leq \frac{1}{\omega_{n-1}(\varepsilon_i L)^{n-1}} \int_{B_{\varepsilon_i L}(x)} e_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i})\, dv_{g_i}  \leq 1+s. 
\end{equation}
Finally, for such $s$ and $b$, by \cite[Proposition 6.2]{LiPariseSarnataro2023} (with $R=\sigma$ and $N=\Theta(P):=\Theta_\mathbf{C}(\textbf{p},z') \in \mathbb{N}$) we can fix $\eta_2 \in (0,1)$ such that\footnote{Note that the second assumption in (iii) of \cite[Proposition 6.2]{LiPariseSarnataro2023} is satisfied for all sufficiently large $i$ as $r^{-(n-1)}\mu_{\tilde{u}_{\tilde{\varepsilon}_i}}(B_r(x_k))$ is of order $r_i$ by the blow-up process. Similarly, the constant $c_6$ in \cite[Lemma 4.12]{LiPariseSarnataro2023} is of order $r_i$ and the constant $c_7$ in \cite[Proposition 4.13]{LiPariseSarnataro2023} is of order $r_i^{1/8}$ in the blow-up process, thus all the assumptions in (ii) of \cite[Proposition 6.2]{LiPariseSarnataro2023} are satisfied for all sufficiently large $i$ as well.}
\[ \begin{array}{l}
\text{(***) For any collection $\{\hat{y}_k\}_{k=1}^{\Theta(P)+1}$ of distinct positive numbers with $\min_{k}|\hat{y}_{k+1}-\hat{y}_k|>3\varepsilon_iL$}\\
\text{and $3\varepsilon_iL<\hat{y}_1<\hat{y}_2<\cdots<\hat{y}_{\Theta(P)+1} < \eta_2 \sigma$ such that whenever $x_k:=(y_1,\hat{y}_k,z) \in C_{\frac{\sigma}{2}} (\textbf{p},z')$ with}\\
\text{$\widehat{E}(u_{\varepsilon_i};B_r(x_k)) \leq \eta_2$ for all $\varepsilon_iL \leq r \leq \sigma$ and all $k$, we have}\\
\end{array}
\]
\begin{equation} 
\label{E:***} 
\sum_{k=1}^{\Theta(P)+1} \frac{1}{\omega_{n-1} (\varepsilon_i L)^{n-1}} \int_{B_{\varepsilon_i L}(x_k)} e_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i})\; dv_{g_i} \leq s + \frac{1+s}{\omega_{n-1} \sigma^{n-1}} \int_{\cup_k B_\sigma(x_k)} e_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i})\; dv_{g_i}.  
\end{equation}
To summarize, for any given $s \in (0,1)$, we first fix $b=b(s) \in (0,\frac{1}{2})$ such that (*) is satisfied. Then we fix $\eta=\min\{\eta_1,\eta_2\} \in (0,1)$ and $L>1$ such that both (**) and (***) hold. We can now sub-divide the points in the intermediate level sets inside $C_{\frac{\sigma}{2}} (\textbf{p},z')$:
\[ C_{\frac{\sigma}{2}} (\textbf{p},z') \cap \{|u_{\varepsilon_i}| \leq 1-b\}=\mathcal{G}^b_i(\textbf{p},z') \cup \mathcal{B}^b_i(\textbf{p},z'), \]
where $x \in \mathcal{G}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')$ belongs to the \emph{good set} if the scale-invariant excess satisfies
\[ \widehat{E}(u_{\varepsilon_i};B_r(x)) \leq \eta \qquad \forall r \in [\varepsilon_i L,\sigma],\]
otherwise $x \in \mathcal{B}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')$ belongs to the \emph{bad set}.

We first prove the following lemma which says that the bad set $\mathcal{B}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')$ has small size with respect to both $\mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}$ and the $\mathcal{L}^{n-1}$-measure of its orthogonal projection onto $P$. This lemma lies at the heart of the remaining arguments in Step 3.

\begin{lemma}
    \label{L:bad-set-estimate}
    Let $\pi_P$ be the orthogonal projection map (with respect to the flat metric) onto $P$. Then,
    \[ \lim_{i \to \infty} \mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}(\mathcal{B}^b_i(\textbf{p},z'))= \lim_{i \to \infty} \mathcal{L}^{n-1}(\pi_P(\mathcal{B}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')))=0. \]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
