    By definition the bad set $\mathcal{B}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')$ is covered by a collection of balls $\mathfrak{B}=\{B_{r_x}(x)\}_{x \in \mathcal{B}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')}$ where $r_x \in [ \varepsilon_i L,\sigma]$ with $\widehat{E}(u_{\varepsilon_i};B_{r_x}(x)) > \eta$. By Besicovitch Covering Theorem, we can choose a sub-collection $\cup_{\ell=1}^{N(n)} \mathfrak{B}_\ell$ covering $\mathcal{B}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')$ such that each $\mathfrak{B}_\ell$ contains mutually disjoint balls. Since $\mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}} \to \Theta(P)\mathcal{H}^{n-1} \mathbin{\vrule height 1.6ex depth 0pt width
0.13ex\vrule height 0.13ex depth 0pt width 1.3ex} P$ as Radon measures in $C_{2\sigma} (\textbf{p},z')$ by Theorem \ref{T:old-main} (1) and \eqref{E:blow-up-argument}, for each $B_{r_x}(x) \in \mathfrak{B}_\ell$, by the choice of $r_x$ such that $\widehat{E}(u_{\varepsilon_i};B_{r_x}(x)) > \eta$, we have for all sufficiently large $i$ that
    \[ \mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}(B_{r_x}(x)) \leq 2 \Theta(P) r_x^{n-1} \leq 2 \Theta(P) \eta^{-1} \int_{B_{r_x}(x) \cap \Omega_i} |\xi_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i})| \; dv_{g_i} +  \big(1-g_i(N_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}, \nabla^{g_i} d_{g_i}^P)^2 \big) \; d\mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}.  \]
    Summing over all the (disjoint!) balls in $\mathfrak{B}_\ell$, we have
    \[ \mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}(\mathfrak{B}_\ell) \leq 2 \Theta(P) \eta^{-1} \int_{\mathfrak{B}_\ell \cap \Omega_i} |\xi_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i})| \; dv_{g_i} +  \big(1-g_i(N_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}, \nabla^{g_i} d_{g_i}^P)^2 \big) \; d\mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}} \]
    which goes to zero as $i \to \infty$ by Theorem \ref{T:old-main} (1) and \eqref{E:blow-up-argument} again. This shows that 
    \[  \lim_{i \to \infty} \mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}(\mathcal{B}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')) \leq N(n) \lim_{i \to \infty} \mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}(\mathfrak{B}_\ell)=0. \]
    Similarly, by considering the collection of projected balls\footnote{Note that the projected balls of $\mathfrak{B}_\ell$ may not be disjoint in $P$, but the same argument still goes through since the balls in $\mathfrak{B}_\ell$ are disjoint before projection.} of $\cup_{\ell=1}^{N(n)} \mathfrak{B}_\ell$ by $\pi_P$ which covers the projected bad set $\pi_P(\mathcal{B}^b_i(\textbf{p},z'))$, we obtain $\lim_{i \to \infty} \mathcal{L}^{n-1}(\pi_P(\mathcal{B}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')))=0$.
\end{proof}

Next, we proceed to estimate in the next two lemmas the size of the exceptional set $S_i(\textbf{p},z')$ as defined in \eqref{E:exceptional-set} based on the decomposition \eqref{E:decompose-S_i}.

\begin{lemma}
\label{L:second-scenario}
$\lim_{i \to \infty} \mathcal{L}^{n-1}(S^{II}_i(\textbf{p},z';b))=0$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
    The assertion follows from Lemma \ref{L:bad-set-estimate} once we show that
    \begin{equation}
        \label{E:lemma-II}
        S^{II}_i(\textbf{p},z';b) \subset \pi_P(\mathcal{B}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')).
    \end{equation}
    To prove \eqref{E:lemma-II}, take any $(y_1,0,z) \in S^{II}_i(\textbf{p},z';b)$, by definition there exists some $y_2' \in [0,\frac{\sigma}{2})$ such that 
    \[ -\frac{1}{2} \leq u_{\varepsilon_i}(y_1,y_2',z) \leq 1-b.\]
    Therefore, $(y_1,y_2',z) \in C_{\frac{\sigma}{2}} (\textbf{p},z') \cap \{|u_{\varepsilon_i}| \leq 1-b\}$. We have two different cases to consider:
    \begin{itemize}
        \item $(y_1,y_2',z) \in \mathcal{G}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')$, in which case $x=(y_1,y_2',z)$ satisfies $\widehat{E}(u_{\varepsilon_i};B_r(x)) \leq \eta$ for all $r \in [\varepsilon_i L,\sigma]$ by definition. By (**), $y_2' \geq 3 \varepsilon_iL$ and inside $B_{\varepsilon_i L}(x)$, $u_{\varepsilon_i}$ is $C^1$-close to the one-dimensional heteroclinic solution $q_{\varepsilon_i}(y_2)$ with $q_{\varepsilon_i}(y_2')=u_{\varepsilon_i}(x)$. By enlarging $L$ depending on $W$ if necessary, we can arrange $\inf_{B_{\varepsilon_i L}(x)} u_{\varepsilon_i} < -\frac{1}{2}$, contradicting \eqref{E:scenario-II-consequence}. Therefore, we have excluded this case.
        \item $(y_1,y_2',z) \in \mathcal{B}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')$, hence $(y_1,0,z) \in \pi_P(\mathcal{B}^b_i(\textbf{p},z'))$ which is our desired conclusion.
    \end{itemize}
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}
    \label{L:first-scenario}
    There exists $s_0=s_0(\Theta(P)) \in (0,1)$ small enough such that for any $s \in (0,s_0)$, we have
    \[ \limsup_{i \to \infty} \mathcal{L}^{n-1}(S^I_i(\textbf{p},z';b)) \leq C(n) s\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
    First of all, by definitions $S^I_i(\textbf{p},z';b)$ and $\pi_P(\mathcal{G}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')$ are disjoint subsets of $P \cap C_{\frac{\sigma}{2}} (\textbf{p},z')$. Therefore, we have
    \begin{equation} 
    \label{E:step-three-proof-a}
    \mathcal{L}^{n-1}(S^I_i(\textbf{p},z';b)) \leq  \omega_{n-1} \left(\frac{\sigma}{2}\right)^{n-1} - \mathcal{L}^{n-1}(\pi_P(\mathcal{G}^b_i(\textbf{p},z'))).
    \end{equation}
    Hence, we need to estimate the second term on the right hand side above. Fix any $|t| \leq 1-b$. Take any $(y_1,0,z) \in \pi_P(\mathcal{G}^b_i(\textbf{p},z'))$, and denote $\{\hat{y}_k\}_{k=1}^K$ to be distinct non-negative numbers such that $\hat{y}_k \leq \eta \sigma$, $x_k=(y_1,\hat{y}_k,z) \in \mathcal{G}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')$ and $u_{\varepsilon_i}(x_k)=t$ for each $k=1,\cdots,K$. We claim that a-priori one must have $K \leq \Theta(P)$. Otherwise, we can take $K=\Theta(P)+1$ and then derive a contradiction below. Roughly speaking, the contradiction comes from the fact that around every $x_k$ in the good set $\mathcal{G}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')$, the smallness of excess at all scales above $O(\varepsilon_i)$ implies good sheet separation and locally at $O(\varepsilon_i)$-scale $u_{\varepsilon_i}$ resembles the one-dimensional heteroclinic solution $q_{\varepsilon_i}(y_2)$. Therefore, having $K \geq \Theta(P)+1$ would contradict the varifold convergence in \eqref{E:blow-up-argument}. 

    Suppose to the contrary that we can take $K=\Theta(P)+1$. Since each $x_k \in \mathcal{G}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')$, (**) implies immediately that all the assumptions in (***) are satisfied for $\{\hat{y}_k\}_{k=1}^{\Theta(P)+1}$. Combining \eqref{E:**} and \eqref{E:***}, taking $i \to \infty$, we obtain
    \[ (\Theta(P)+1)(1-s) \leq s + \frac{1+s}{\omega_{n-1}\sigma^{n-1}} \|\mathbf{C}\|\left(\bigcup_{k=1}^{\Theta(P)+1} \overline{B_\sigma(x_k)} \right) \leq s+ (1+s) \Theta(P) \]
    which will result in a contradiction provided that $s < \frac{1}{2(\Theta(P)+1)}$.

    To finish the proof of the lemma, we perform a standard change of variable
    \[ w_{u_{\varepsilon_i}} := \Phi \circ u_{\varepsilon_i} \qquad \text{ where } \Phi(t):=\int_0^t \sqrt{2W(\tau)} \; d\tau.\]
    Note that $\nabla w_{\varepsilon_i}=\sqrt{2W(u_{\varepsilon_i})} \, \nabla u_{\varepsilon_i}$ and (recall \eqref{E:energy-density-discrepancy})
    \[ \left| e_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i}) - \frac{|\nabla w_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}|}{h_0} \right| \leq |\xi_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i})|.\]
    By Theorem \ref{T:old-main} (1), we have 
    \begin{equation} 
    \label{E:step-three-last-equation}
    \lim_{i \to \infty} \int_{C_{\frac{\sigma}{2}} (\textbf{p},z')} \frac{|\nabla w_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}|}{h_0} \; dv_{g_i}= \|\mathbf{C}\|(C_{\frac{\sigma}{2}} (\textbf{p},z'))= \Theta(P) \omega_{n-1} \left( \frac{\sigma}{2} \right)^{n-1}. 
    \end{equation}
    On the other hand, using (*), Lemma \ref{L:bad-set-estimate}, the co-area formula and \eqref{E:energy-constant}, 
    \begin{eqnarray*}
    \lim_{i \to \infty} \int_{C_{\frac{\sigma}{2}} (\textbf{p},z')} \frac{|\nabla w_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}|}{h_0} \; dv_{g_i} & \leq & s + \liminf_{i \to \infty} \int_{\mathcal{G}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')} \frac{|\nabla w_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}|}{h_0} \; dv_{g_i}  \\
    & \leq & s + \liminf_{i \to \infty} \int_{-1+b}^{1-b} \mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\{u_{\varepsilon_i}=t\} \cap \mathcal{G}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')) \frac{\sqrt{2W(t)}}{h_0} \; dt \\
    & \leq & s+ (1+s) \Theta(P) \liminf_{i \to \infty} \mathcal{L}^{n-1}(\pi_P(\mathcal{G}^b_i(\textbf{p},z'))).
    \end{eqnarray*}
    The last inequality is due to the observation (c.f. \cite[(4.11)]{Tonegawa2012}) that around any $x \in \mathcal{G}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')$ which lies on a regular level set of $u_{\varepsilon_i}$, since $u_{\varepsilon_i}$ is $C^1$-close to the one-dimensional heteroclinic solution $q_{\varepsilon_i}(y_2)$ inside $B_{\varepsilon_i L}(x)$, the level set $\{u_{\varepsilon_i}=u_{\varepsilon_i}(x)\}$ can be written as the graph of a function over $\pi_P(B_{\varepsilon_i L}(x))$ with gradient approaching zero less than  as $i \to \infty$. Combining the above with \eqref{E:step-three-last-equation}, and taking limsup on both sides of \eqref{E:step-three-proof-a}, we obtain
    \[  \limsup_{i \to \infty} \mathcal{L}^{n-1}(S^I_i(\textbf{p},z';b)) \leq \left( \omega_{n-1}\left( \frac{\sigma}{2} \right)^{n-1} + \frac{1}{\Theta(P)} \right) s, \]
    which proves the assertion in the lemma.
\end{proof}

By Lemma \ref{L:second-scenario} and \ref{L:first-scenario}, together with \eqref{E:decompose-S_i}, we have
\[\lim_{i \to \infty} \mathcal{L}^{n-1}(S_i(\textbf{p},z')) \leq C(n)s \qquad \text{for all $s \in (0,s_0)$,} \]
hence we obtain the desired conclusion \eqref{E:step-3-proof-main-estimate}.        
\begin{comment}
            
                 
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     
               
             
                                                                                                      
           
                                                                                            
                

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  
              
\end{comment}

\vspace{1ex}

\textit{Step 4: Controlling in an integral sense the total turning angle of the cross sections of the intermediate level sets of $u_{\varepsilon}$ and the final contradiction argument.}

\vspace{1ex}

In what follows, we will use $B^k_r$ to denote the Euclidean ball of radius $r>0$ centred at the origin. Let $\pi_{S(\mathbf{C})}$ be the orthogonal projection map (with respect to the flat metric) onto the spine $S(\mathbf{C}) \cong \mathbb{R}^{n-2}$. We first establish a lemma saying that generic level sets of $u_{\varepsilon_i}$ satisfy some nice properties.

\begin{lemma}
    \label{L:nice-level-sets-are-generic}
    For $\mathcal{L}^1$-a.e. $t \in (-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})$, the following statements hold:
    \begin{itemize}
        \item[(1)] For every $i \in \mathbb{N}$, $t$ is a regular value for $u_{\varepsilon_i}$ restricted on the open\footnote{Note that each $\mathcal{I}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}$ is a relatively closed subset of $B^{n-2}_1$ by definition.} set $B^2_1 \times (B^{n-2}_1\setminus \mathcal{I}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}})$, thus the level set $\{u_{\varepsilon_i}=t\}$ is a $C^3$ hypersurface in $\Omega_i$ with free boundary on $\partial \Omega_i$. 
        \item[(2)] Over the ``nice part'' $\mathfrak{N}_i \subset B^2_1 \times B^{n-2}_1 \subset B^n_2$ defined by
        \[ \mathfrak{N}_i:=\pi_{S(\mathbf{C})}^{-1}(B^{n-2}_1\setminus (\mathcal{I}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}} \cup \mathcal{N}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}})), \]
        the level hypersurfaces $\{u_{\varepsilon_i}=t\}$ have finite area in the liminf sense, i.e.
        \begin{equation}
            \label{E:finite-area-level-set}
        \liminf_{i \to \infty} \mathcal{H}^{n-1}_{g_i}(\{u_{\varepsilon_i} = t \} \cap \mathfrak{N}_i) < \infty;
        \end{equation} 
        and their enhanced second fundamental form are converging to zero in the $L^2$-sense, i.e.
        \begin{equation}
            \label{E:2ff-converges-to-zero}
            \liminf_{i \to \infty} \int_{\{u_{\varepsilon_i}=t\} \cap \mathfrak{N}_i} |\mathcal{A}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}|^2 \; d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}_{g_i} = 0.
        \end{equation}
    \end{itemize}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(1) is a direct consequence of Sard's theorem. To prove (2), first note that by the definition of $\mathcal{I}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}$ (recall \eqref{E:step-2-proof}), for any $z \in B^{n-2}_1\setminus \mathcal{I}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}$, there exists $r_z>0$ such that
\begin{equation} 
\label{E:non-degeneracy}
|\nabla u_{\varepsilon_i}| \geq \frac{c}{\varepsilon_i} \qquad \text{ on $B^2_1 \times B^{n-2}_{r_z}(z) \cap \left\{|u_{\varepsilon_i}|\leq \frac{1}{2}\right\} $.}
\end{equation}
Using co-area formula and \eqref{E:non-degeneracy}, 
\[ \int_{-\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}} \mathcal{H}^{n-1}_{g_i}(\{u_{\varepsilon_i} = t \} \cap \mathfrak{N}_i) \; dt = \int_{\left\{|u_{\varepsilon_i}|\leq \frac{1}{2}\right\} \cap \mathfrak{N}_i} |\nabla u_{\varepsilon_i}|\; dv_{g_i} \leq \frac{h_0}{c} \mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}(B^n_2), \]
which implies \eqref{E:finite-area-level-set} by letting $i \to \infty$ using Fatou's lemma. For \eqref{E:2ff-converges-to-zero}, using again co-area formula and \eqref{E:non-degeneracy}, 
\[ \int_{-\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}} \int_{\{u_{\varepsilon_i}=t\} \cap \mathfrak{N}_i} |\mathcal{A}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}|^2 \; d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}_{g_i} \; dt= \int_{\left\{|u_{\varepsilon_i}|\leq \frac{1}{2}\right\} \cap \mathfrak{N}_i} |\mathcal{A}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}|^2 |\nabla u_{\varepsilon_i}| \; dv_{g_i} \leq \frac{h_0}{c} \int_{B^n_2}|\mathcal{A}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}|^2 \; d\mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}.\]
The desired assertion follows by taking $i \to \infty$ and using Fatou's lemma together with \eqref{E:blow-up-argument}.
\end{proof}

Applying an elementary geometric lemma \cite[Lemma 4.3]{Tonegawa2012}\footnote{The proof there works verbatim in the Riemannian setting with boundary.}, Lemma \ref{L:nice-level-sets-are-generic} with another use of Fatou's lemma implies that for $\mathcal{L}^1$-a.e. $t \in (-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})$ such that (1) and (2) in Lemma \ref{L:nice-level-sets-are-generic} holds, 
\[ \liminf_{i \to \infty} \int_{\ell_z} |\kappa^i_{z}|\; ds_{g_i} = 0  \]
for $\mathcal{L}^{n-2}$-a.e. $z \in B^{n-2}_1\setminus (\mathcal{I}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}} \cup \mathcal{N}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}})$ which is also a regular value of $\pi_{S(\mathbf{C})}$ restricted on the level hypersurface $\{u_{\varepsilon_i}=t\}$ in $B^2_1 \times (B^{n-2}_1\setminus \mathcal{I}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}})$. Here, $\ell^i_z:=\pi_{S(\mathbf{C})}^{-1}(z) \cap \{u_{\varepsilon_i}=t\}$ is a $C^3$ planar curve contained in $B^2_1 \times \{z\}$, and $\kappa^i_z$ is the geodesic curvature of the planar curve $\ell^i_z$ with respect to $g_i$. Since $\lim_{i \to \infty} \mathcal{L}^{n-2}(\mathcal{I}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}} \cup \mathcal{N}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}})= 0$ by \eqref{E:step-2-proof} and \eqref{E:step-3-proof}, we can choose a sequence of $z_i \in  B^{n-2}_1\setminus (\mathcal{I}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}} \cup \mathcal{N}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}})$ which is a regular value of $\pi_{S(\mathbf{C})}$ restricted on the level hypersurface $\{u_{\varepsilon_i}=t\}$ in $B^2_1 \times (B^{n-2}_1\setminus \mathcal{I}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}})$ and, possibly after passing to a subsequence, 
\begin{equation}
    \label{E:final-contradiction}
\lim_{i \to \infty} \int_{\ell^i_{z_i}} |\kappa^i_{z_i}|\; ds_{g_i} = 0. 
\end{equation} 

Finally, we describe the contradiction arguments. Note that each $\ell^i_{z_i}$ is a non-empty union of disjoint, connected, properly embedded planar curves in $\Omega_i \cap (B^2_1 \times \{z_i\})$ with (possibly empty) free boundary in $\partial \Omega_i \cap (B^2_1 \times \{z_i\})$. Moreover, by the definition of $\mathcal{N}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}$, $\ell^i_{z_i}$ must intersect every one of the disjoint disks $B^2_{\sigma}(\textbf{p}_j) \times \{z_i\}$ for all $j=1,\cdots,N$. Since $N \geq 2$ and $\operatorname{spt}\|\mathbf{C}\| \neq \partial \mathbb{R}^n_+$, for all sufficiently large $i$ (recall \eqref{E:ambient-convergence}), we have one of the following cases to consider: (in what follows, we call a component ``unbounded'' if it is not contained in a compact subset of $B^2_1 \times \{z_i\}$)
\begin{itemize}
    \item there exists a closed (i.e. without boundary) component of $\ell^i_{z_i}$, which would result in $\int_{\ell^i_{z_i}} |\kappa^i_{z_i}|\; ds_{g_i} \geq 1.99\pi$.
    \item there exists a component of $\ell^i_{z_i}$ with two free boundary points at $\partial \Omega_i \cap (B^2_1 \times \{z_i\})$, which would result in $\int_{\ell^i_{z_i}} |\kappa^i_{z_i}|\; ds_{g_i} \geq 0.99\pi$.
    \item there exists a component of $\ell^i_{z_i}$ that intersects both $B^2_{\sigma}(\textbf{p}_{j_1}) \times \{z_i\}$ and $B^2_{\sigma}(\textbf{p}_{j_2}) \times \{z_i\}$ for some $j_1\neq j_2$ such that one of the $\textbf{p}_{j_1}$ or $\textbf{p}_{j_2}$ does not lie on $\partial \mathbb{R}^2_+$, by the Hausdorff convergence of the level sets in Theorem \ref{T:old-main} (2), we must have 
    \[ \int_{\ell^i_{z_i}} |\kappa^i_{z_i}|\; ds_{g_i} \geq c(\textbf{p}_{j_1},\textbf{p}_{j_2},\sigma)>0.\]
    \item there exists an unbounded component of $\ell^i_{z_i}$ that intersects only one $B^2_{\sigma}(\textbf{p}_{j}) \times \{z_i\}$ for some $\textbf{p}_j \neq (0,\frac{1}{2})$ which has exactly one free boundary point at $\partial \Omega_i \cap (B^2_1 \times \{z_i\})$, in this case we must have
    \[ \int_{\ell^i_{z_i}} |\kappa^i_{z_i}|\; ds_{g_i} \geq c(\textbf{p}_j,\sigma)>0.\]
    \item there exists an unbounded component of $\ell^i_{z_i}$ that intersects only one $B^2_{\sigma}(\textbf{p}_{j}) \times \{z_i\}$ without boundary points, which would again result in $\int_{\ell^i_{z_i}} |\kappa^i_{z_i}|\; ds_{g_i} \geq 0.99\pi$.
\end{itemize}
Therefore, in all of the above cases, we always have (recall that $\sigma$ only depends on $\mathbf{C}$ as well)
\[ \int_{\ell^i_{z_i}} |\kappa^i_{z_i}|\; ds_{g_i} \geq c(\mathbf{C})>0\]
which clearly contradicts \eqref{E:final-contradiction}. This finishes the proof of excluding boundary classical tangent cones of Type I. The proof for Type II is very similar and the details are left to the reader.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- remark-32: body_md -->
Note that by standard elliptic estimates (c.f. \cite[Lemma 4.7]{LiPariseSarnataro2023}), we have the uniform gradient estimate $\varepsilon_i | \nabla^{g_i} u_{\varepsilon_i}(y',z')|_{g_i} \leq C$ for some large enough $C>0$. Therefore, \eqref{E:step-2-proof} amounts to showing that in a limiting measure-theoretic sense, inside the transition layer $\{|u_{\varepsilon_i}|\leq \frac{1}{2}\}$, the gradient of $u_{\varepsilon_i}$ is indeed of order $\frac{1}{\varepsilon_i}$.
<!-- end remark-32 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{L:bad-set-estimate}
    Let $\pi_P$ be the orthogonal projection map (with respect to the flat metric) onto $P$. Then,
    \[ \lim_{i \to \infty} \mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}(\mathcal{B}^b_i(\textbf{p},z'))= \lim_{i \to \infty} \mathcal{L}^{n-1}(\pi_P(\mathcal{B}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')))=0. \]
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
By definition the bad set $\mathcal{B}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')$ is covered by a collection of balls $\mathfrak{B}=\{B_{r_x}(x)\}_{x \in \mathcal{B}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')}$ where $r_x \in [ \varepsilon_i L,\sigma]$ with $\widehat{E}(u_{\varepsilon_i};B_{r_x}(x)) > \eta$. By Besicovitch Covering Theorem, we can choose a sub-collection $\cup_{\ell=1}^{N(n)} \mathfrak{B}_\ell$ covering $\mathcal{B}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')$ such that each $\mathfrak{B}_\ell$ contains mutually disjoint balls. Since $\mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}} \to \Theta(P)\mathcal{H}^{n-1} \mathbin{\vrule height 1.6ex depth 0pt width
0.13ex\vrule height 0.13ex depth 0pt width 1.3ex} P$ as Radon measures in $C_{2\sigma} (\textbf{p},z')$ by Theorem \ref{T:old-main} (1) and \eqref{E:blow-up-argument}, for each $B_{r_x}(x) \in \mathfrak{B}_\ell$, by the choice of $r_x$ such that $\widehat{E}(u_{\varepsilon_i};B_{r_x}(x)) > \eta$, we have for all sufficiently large $i$ that
    \[ \mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}(B_{r_x}(x)) \leq 2 \Theta(P) r_x^{n-1} \leq 2 \Theta(P) \eta^{-1} \int_{B_{r_x}(x) \cap \Omega_i} |\xi_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i})| \; dv_{g_i} +  \big(1-g_i(N_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}, \nabla^{g_i} d_{g_i}^P)^2 \big) \; d\mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}.  \]
    Summing over all the (disjoint!) balls in $\mathfrak{B}_\ell$, we have
    \[ \mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}(\mathfrak{B}_\ell) \leq 2 \Theta(P) \eta^{-1} \int_{\mathfrak{B}_\ell \cap \Omega_i} |\xi_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i})| \; dv_{g_i} +  \big(1-g_i(N_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}, \nabla^{g_i} d_{g_i}^P)^2 \big) \; d\mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}} \]
    which goes to zero as $i \to \infty$ by Theorem \ref{T:old-main} (1) and \eqref{E:blow-up-argument} again. This shows that 
    \[  \lim_{i \to \infty} \mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}(\mathcal{B}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')) \leq N(n) \lim_{i \to \infty} \mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}(\mathfrak{B}_\ell)=0. \]
    Similarly, by considering the collection of projected balls\footnote{Note that the projected balls of $\mathfrak{B}_\ell$ may not be disjoint in $P$, but the same argument still goes through since the balls in $\mathfrak{B}_\ell$ are disjoint before projection.} of $\cup_{\ell=1}^{N(n)} \mathfrak{B}_\ell$ by $\pi_P$ which covers the projected bad set $\pi_P(\mathcal{B}^b_i(\textbf{p},z'))$, we obtain $\lim_{i \to \infty} \mathcal{L}^{n-1}(\pi_P(\mathcal{B}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')))=0$.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{L:second-scenario}
$\lim_{i \to \infty} \mathcal{L}^{n-1}(S^{II}_i(\textbf{p},z';b))=0$.
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
The assertion follows from Lemma \ref{L:bad-set-estimate} once we show that
    \begin{equation}
        \label{E:lemma-II}
        S^{II}_i(\textbf{p},z';b) \subset \pi_P(\mathcal{B}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')).
    \end{equation}
    To prove \eqref{E:lemma-II}, take any $(y_1,0,z) \in S^{II}_i(\textbf{p},z';b)$, by definition there exists some $y_2' \in [0,\frac{\sigma}{2})$ such that 
    \[ -\frac{1}{2} \leq u_{\varepsilon_i}(y_1,y_2',z) \leq 1-b.\]
    Therefore, $(y_1,y_2',z) \in C_{\frac{\sigma}{2}} (\textbf{p},z') \cap \{|u_{\varepsilon_i}| \leq 1-b\}$. We have two different cases to consider:
    \begin{itemize}
        \item $(y_1,y_2',z) \in \mathcal{G}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')$, in which case $x=(y_1,y_2',z)$ satisfies $\widehat{E}(u_{\varepsilon_i};B_r(x)) \leq \eta$ for all $r \in [\varepsilon_i L,\sigma]$ by definition. By (**), $y_2' \geq 3 \varepsilon_iL$ and inside $B_{\varepsilon_i L}(x)$, $u_{\varepsilon_i}$ is $C^1$-close to the one-dimensional heteroclinic solution $q_{\varepsilon_i}(y_2)$ with $q_{\varepsilon_i}(y_2')=u_{\varepsilon_i}(x)$. By enlarging $L$ depending on $W$ if necessary, we can arrange $\inf_{B_{\varepsilon_i L}(x)} u_{\varepsilon_i} < -\frac{1}{2}$, contradicting \eqref{E:scenario-II-consequence}. Therefore, we have excluded this case.
        \item $(y_1,y_2',z) \in \mathcal{B}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')$, hence $(y_1,0,z) \in \pi_P(\mathcal{B}^b_i(\textbf{p},z'))$ which is our desired conclusion.
    \end{itemize}
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{L:first-scenario}
    There exists $s_0=s_0(\Theta(P)) \in (0,1)$ small enough such that for any $s \in (0,s_0)$, we have
    \[ \limsup_{i \to \infty} \mathcal{L}^{n-1}(S^I_i(\textbf{p},z';b)) \leq C(n) s\]
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
First of all, by definitions $S^I_i(\textbf{p},z';b)$ and $\pi_P(\mathcal{G}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')$ are disjoint subsets of $P \cap C_{\frac{\sigma}{2}} (\textbf{p},z')$. Therefore, we have
    \begin{equation} 
    \label{E:step-three-proof-a}
    \mathcal{L}^{n-1}(S^I_i(\textbf{p},z';b)) \leq  \omega_{n-1} \left(\frac{\sigma}{2}\right)^{n-1} - \mathcal{L}^{n-1}(\pi_P(\mathcal{G}^b_i(\textbf{p},z'))).
    \end{equation}
    Hence, we need to estimate the second term on the right hand side above. Fix any $|t| \leq 1-b$. Take any $(y_1,0,z) \in \pi_P(\mathcal{G}^b_i(\textbf{p},z'))$, and denote $\{\hat{y}_k\}_{k=1}^K$ to be distinct non-negative numbers such that $\hat{y}_k \leq \eta \sigma$, $x_k=(y_1,\hat{y}_k,z) \in \mathcal{G}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')$ and $u_{\varepsilon_i}(x_k)=t$ for each $k=1,\cdots,K$. We claim that a-priori one must have $K \leq \Theta(P)$. Otherwise, we can take $K=\Theta(P)+1$ and then derive a contradiction below. Roughly speaking, the contradiction comes from the fact that around every $x_k$ in the good set $\mathcal{G}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')$, the smallness of excess at all scales above $O(\varepsilon_i)$ implies good sheet separation and locally at $O(\varepsilon_i)$-scale $u_{\varepsilon_i}$ resembles the one-dimensional heteroclinic solution $q_{\varepsilon_i}(y_2)$. Therefore, having $K \geq \Theta(P)+1$ would contradict the varifold convergence in \eqref{E:blow-up-argument}. 

    Suppose to the contrary that we can take $K=\Theta(P)+1$. Since each $x_k \in \mathcal{G}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')$, (**) implies immediately that all the assumptions in (***) are satisfied for $\{\hat{y}_k\}_{k=1}^{\Theta(P)+1}$. Combining \eqref{E:**} and \eqref{E:***}, taking $i \to \infty$, we obtain
    \[ (\Theta(P)+1)(1-s) \leq s + \frac{1+s}{\omega_{n-1}\sigma^{n-1}} \|\mathbf{C}\|\left(\bigcup_{k=1}^{\Theta(P)+1} \overline{B_\sigma(x_k)} \right) \leq s+ (1+s) \Theta(P) \]
    which will result in a contradiction provided that $s < \frac{1}{2(\Theta(P)+1)}$.

    To finish the proof of the lemma, we perform a standard change of variable
    \[ w_{u_{\varepsilon_i}} := \Phi \circ u_{\varepsilon_i} \qquad \text{ where } \Phi(t):=\int_0^t \sqrt{2W(\tau)} \; d\tau.\]
    Note that $\nabla w_{\varepsilon_i}=\sqrt{2W(u_{\varepsilon_i})} \, \nabla u_{\varepsilon_i}$ and (recall \eqref{E:energy-density-discrepancy})
    \[ \left| e_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i}) - \frac{|\nabla w_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}|}{h_0} \right| \leq |\xi_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i})|.\]
    By Theorem \ref{T:old-main} (1), we have 
    \begin{equation} 
    \label{E:step-three-last-equation}
    \lim_{i \to \infty} \int_{C_{\frac{\sigma}{2}} (\textbf{p},z')} \frac{|\nabla w_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}|}{h_0} \; dv_{g_i}= \|\mathbf{C}\|(C_{\frac{\sigma}{2}} (\textbf{p},z'))= \Theta(P) \omega_{n-1} \left( \frac{\sigma}{2} \right)^{n-1}. 
    \end{equation}
    On the other hand, using (*), Lemma \ref{L:bad-set-estimate}, the co-area formula and \eqref{E:energy-constant}, 
    \begin{eqnarray*}
    \lim_{i \to \infty} \int_{C_{\frac{\sigma}{2}} (\textbf{p},z')} \frac{|\nabla w_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}|}{h_0} \; dv_{g_i} & \leq & s + \liminf_{i \to \infty} \int_{\mathcal{G}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')} \frac{|\nabla w_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}|}{h_0} \; dv_{g_i}  \\
    & \leq & s + \liminf_{i \to \infty} \int_{-1+b}^{1-b} \mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\{u_{\varepsilon_i}=t\} \cap \mathcal{G}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')) \frac{\sqrt{2W(t)}}{h_0} \; dt \\
    & \leq & s+ (1+s) \Theta(P) \liminf_{i \to \infty} \mathcal{L}^{n-1}(\pi_P(\mathcal{G}^b_i(\textbf{p},z'))).
    \end{eqnarray*}
    The last inequality is due to the observation (c.f. \cite[(4.11)]{Tonegawa2012}) that around any $x \in \mathcal{G}^b_i(\textbf{p},z')$ which lies on a regular level set of $u_{\varepsilon_i}$, since $u_{\varepsilon_i}$ is $C^1$-close to the one-dimensional heteroclinic solution $q_{\varepsilon_i}(y_2)$ inside $B_{\varepsilon_i L}(x)$, the level set $\{u_{\varepsilon_i}=u_{\varepsilon_i}(x)\}$ can be written as the graph of a function over $\pi_P(B_{\varepsilon_i L}(x))$ with gradient approaching zero less than  as $i \to \infty$. Combining the above with \eqref{E:step-three-last-equation}, and taking limsup on both sides of \eqref{E:step-three-proof-a}, we obtain
    \[  \limsup_{i \to \infty} \mathcal{L}^{n-1}(S^I_i(\textbf{p},z';b)) \leq \left( \omega_{n-1}\left( \frac{\sigma}{2} \right)^{n-1} + \frac{1}{\Theta(P)} \right) s, \]
    which proves the assertion in the lemma.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{L:nice-level-sets-are-generic}
    For $\mathcal{L}^1$-a.e. $t \in (-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})$, the following statements hold:
    \begin{itemize}
        \item[(1)] For every $i \in \mathbb{N}$, $t$ is a regular value for $u_{\varepsilon_i}$ restricted on the open\footnote{Note that each $\mathcal{I}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}$ is a relatively closed subset of $B^{n-2}_1$ by definition.} set $B^2_1 \times (B^{n-2}_1\setminus \mathcal{I}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}})$, thus the level set $\{u_{\varepsilon_i}=t\}$ is a $C^3$ hypersurface in $\Omega_i$ with free boundary on $\partial \Omega_i$. 
        \item[(2)] Over the ``nice part'' $\mathfrak{N}_i \subset B^2_1 \times B^{n-2}_1 \subset B^n_2$ defined by
        \[ \mathfrak{N}_i:=\pi_{S(\mathbf{C})}^{-1}(B^{n-2}_1\setminus (\mathcal{I}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}} \cup \mathcal{N}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}})), \]
        the level hypersurfaces $\{u_{\varepsilon_i}=t\}$ have finite area in the liminf sense, i.e.
        \begin{equation}
            \label{E:finite-area-level-set}
        \liminf_{i \to \infty} \mathcal{H}^{n-1}_{g_i}(\{u_{\varepsilon_i} = t \} \cap \mathfrak{N}_i) < \infty;
        \end{equation} 
        and their enhanced second fundamental form are converging to zero in the $L^2$-sense, i.e.
        \begin{equation}
            \label{E:2ff-converges-to-zero}
            \liminf_{i \to \infty} \int_{\{u_{\varepsilon_i}=t\} \cap \mathfrak{N}_i} |\mathcal{A}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}|^2 \; d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}_{g_i} = 0.
        \end{equation}
    \end{itemize}
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
(1) is a direct consequence of Sard's theorem. To prove (2), first note that by the definition of $\mathcal{I}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}$ (recall \eqref{E:step-2-proof}), for any $z \in B^{n-2}_1\setminus \mathcal{I}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}$, there exists $r_z>0$ such that
\begin{equation} 
\label{E:non-degeneracy}
|\nabla u_{\varepsilon_i}| \geq \frac{c}{\varepsilon_i} \qquad \text{ on $B^2_1 \times B^{n-2}_{r_z}(z) \cap \left\{|u_{\varepsilon_i}|\leq \frac{1}{2}\right\} $.}
\end{equation}
Using co-area formula and \eqref{E:non-degeneracy}, 
\[ \int_{-\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}} \mathcal{H}^{n-1}_{g_i}(\{u_{\varepsilon_i} = t \} \cap \mathfrak{N}_i) \; dt = \int_{\left\{|u_{\varepsilon_i}|\leq \frac{1}{2}\right\} \cap \mathfrak{N}_i} |\nabla u_{\varepsilon_i}|\; dv_{g_i} \leq \frac{h_0}{c} \mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}(B^n_2), \]
which implies \eqref{E:finite-area-level-set} by letting $i \to \infty$ using Fatou's lemma. For \eqref{E:2ff-converges-to-zero}, using again co-area formula and \eqref{E:non-degeneracy}, 
\[ \int_{-\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}} \int_{\{u_{\varepsilon_i}=t\} \cap \mathfrak{N}_i} |\mathcal{A}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}|^2 \; d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}_{g_i} \; dt= \int_{\left\{|u_{\varepsilon_i}|\leq \frac{1}{2}\right\} \cap \mathfrak{N}_i} |\mathcal{A}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}|^2 |\nabla u_{\varepsilon_i}| \; dv_{g_i} \leq \frac{h_0}{c} \int_{B^n_2}|\mathcal{A}_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}|^2 \; d\mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}.\]
The desired assertion follows by taking $i \to \infty$ and using Fatou's lemma together with \eqref{E:blow-up-argument}.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- remark-33: body_md -->
We will mainly focus on the homogeneous case $\Lambda_0=0$. When $\Lambda_0\neq0$, we can consider as in \cite{LiPariseSarnataro2023} the following modified (non-negative) potential
\[
\widetilde W_\varepsilon(s)=W(s)-\varepsilon\lambda_\varepsilon s+\varepsilon C_0\Lambda_0.
\]
and the following modified normalized energy density and discrepancy function (recall \eqref{E:energy-density-discrepancy}):
\begin{equation}
\label{E:modified-densities}
\widetilde e_\varepsilon (u_\varepsilon):=e_\varepsilon (u_\varepsilon)+f_\varepsilon(u_\varepsilon),
\qquad
\widetilde\xi_\varepsilon (u_\varepsilon):=\xi_\varepsilon (u_\varepsilon)-f_\varepsilon (u_\varepsilon).
\end{equation}
where $f_\varepsilon(u_\varepsilon):=h_0^{-1}(-\lambda_\varepsilon u_\varepsilon+C_0\Lambda_0)$ satisfies $0\leq f_\varepsilon\leq2h_0^{-1}C_0\Lambda_0$. Thus the same computations below apply to $\widetilde e_\varepsilon$ and $\widetilde\xi_\varepsilon$ in place of $e_\varepsilon$ and $\xi_\varepsilon$.  Note that the associated varifold $V^{u_\varepsilon}$ defined in \eqref{E:varifold-def} is independent of the potential term and hence the limit-interface $V$ would be the same even if we consider the modified quantities as above.
<!-- end remark-33 -->
<!-- proposition-6: body_md -->
\label{P:reflected-Hausdorff}
For any $b\in(0,1)$, the subsets $\{|u_i|\leq1-b\}$ locally converge in the Hausdorff sense to $\operatorname{spt}\|V\|$ in $\overline\Omega\cap U$.
<!-- end proposition-6 -->
<!-- proposition-7: body_md -->
\label{P:reflected-monotonicity}
There exists $C=C(n,k_0,\kappa_0,\Lambda_0)\geq0$ such that the following holds: for every $a\in \overline\Omega \cap U_{\frac{1}{4\kappa_0}}(\partial \Omega)$ and almost every $\rho \in (0,\frac{1}{4\kappa_0})$,
\begin{equation}
\label{E:reflected-mono}
\frac{d}{d\rho}\left(e^{C\rho}\rho^{1-n}\mathcal E_\varepsilon(\rho,a)\right)
\geq e^{C\rho}\rho^{-n}D_\varepsilon(\rho,a).
\end{equation}
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      
On the other hand, for any $a \in \Omega \cap U_{\frac{1}{4\kappa_0}}(\partial \Omega)$ and almost every $0<\rho<\min\{\frac{1}{4\kappa_0},\operatorname{dist}_g(a,\partial \Omega)\}$, we have
\begin{equation}
\label{E:interior-mono-1}
\frac{d}{d\rho}\left(e^{C\rho}\rho^{1-n}
\int_{B_\rho(a)}e_\varepsilon\,d\mathrm{vol}_g\right)
\geq e^{C\rho}\rho^{-n}\int_{B_\rho(a)}(-\xi_\varepsilon)\,d\mathrm{vol}_g.
\end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-7 -->
<!-- remark-34: body_md -->
The monotonicity formulas above are stated for the homogeneous case, i.e. $\Lambda_0=0$. When $\Lambda_0 \neq 0$, they hold with $e_\varepsilon$ and $\xi_\varepsilon$ replaced by $\widetilde e_\varepsilon$ and $\widetilde\xi_\varepsilon$ from \eqref{E:modified-densities}.
<!-- end remark-34 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
Let $\mu_i=\mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}=\|V^{u_{\varepsilon_i}}\|$ be the Radon measures as defined in \eqref{E:mu}. Note that $\mu_i \to \|V\|$ as Radon measures on $U$. By \cite[Theorem 3.3(2)]{LiPariseSarnataro2023}, we already have the required Hausdorff convergence away from the boundary $\partial \Omega$. Therefore, we now focus on the additional arguments needed to conclude the Hausdorff convergence up to the boundary using Proposition \ref{P:reflected-monotonicity}. 

Fix $b\in(0,1)$ and work on a relatively compact subset of $U$, shrinking the admissible radii if needed. The local gradient estimate and the discrepancy and energy estimates in \cite[Section 4.2 and Propositions 4.10, 4.13, 4.15]{LiPariseSarnataro2023} give
\begin{equation}
\label{E:estimates-for-Hausdorff}
\varepsilon|\nabla u_\varepsilon|\leq C,\qquad
\xi_\varepsilon^+\leq C\varepsilon^{-4/5},\qquad
\int_{B_r(a)\cap\Omega}e_\varepsilon\,d\mathrm{vol}_g\leq Cr^{n-1}
\end{equation}
for $\varepsilon\leq r\leq r_0$ and all centres under consideration. To obtain the last estimate for a ball meeting the boundary whose centre is interior, include it in $B_{2r}(\pi(a))\cap\Omega$; balls disjoint from the boundary are covered by the interior estimate. The integral discrepancy estimate then yields
\begin{equation}
\label{E:integrated-discrepancy-Hausdorff}
r^{-n}\int_{B_r(a)\cap\Omega}\xi_\varepsilon^+\, d\mathrm{vol}_g\leq Cr^{-7/8}
\qquad \text{for any $c\varepsilon^{4/5}\leq r\leq r_0$}.
\end{equation}
Here $c>0$ can be any fixed constant, cf. \cite[Proposition 4.13]{LiPariseSarnataro2023}.

For the reflected sets, $t\leq\widetilde r$ and $\operatorname{dist}_g(x,\mathcal R(x))=2t$ imply
\begin{equation}
\label{E:reflected-ball-comparison}
\widetilde B_r(a)\subset B_{3r}(a)\cap\Omega.
\end{equation}
Thus the same upper bounds apply to the reflected energy and positive discrepancy, after changing the constants.

Suppose now that $|u_\varepsilon(a)|\leq1-b$. The gradient bound in \eqref{E:estimates-for-Hausdorff}, positivity of $W$ away from its wells, and the local volume lower bound give $\varepsilon^{1-n}\mathcal E_\varepsilon(\varepsilon,a)\geq\theta_b>0.$ Apply Proposition \ref{P:reflected-monotonicity} from $\varepsilon$ to $s_\varepsilon=c\varepsilon^{4/5}$. Choosing $c$ sufficiently small preserves a positive lower bound at $s_\varepsilon$. From $s_\varepsilon$ to a fixed small radius, use \eqref{E:integrated-discrepancy-Hausdorff}. Its integral is bounded by $Cr^{1/8}$, so, after decreasing $r_0$, we obtain $\mathcal E_\varepsilon(r,a)\geq\theta r^{n-1}$ for $\varepsilon\leq r\leq r_0.$ Finally, \eqref{E:reflected-ball-comparison} gives the lower bound for the original energy measure:
\begin{equation}
\label{E:transition-density-lower}
\mu_i(B_r(a))\geq\theta_0r^{n-1}
\quad\text{if }|u_i(a)|\leq1-b,\qquad
3\varepsilon_i\leq r\leq r_0.
\end{equation}
The constants are locally uniform in $a$ and independent of $i$ for all sufficiently large $i$.

Consequently, if $a_i\in\{|u_i|\leq1-b\}$ and $a_i\to x\in\partial\Omega\cap U$, then $x\in\operatorname{spt}\mu$. Indeed, for each fixed sufficiently small $r>0$ and all large $i$, $B_{r/4}(a_i)\subset\overline B_{r/2}(x)$. By \eqref{E:transition-density-lower} and weak convergence,
\[
\mu(\overline B_{r/2}(x))\geq\limsup_{i\to\infty}\mu_i(\overline B_{r/2}(x))
\geq\theta_0(r/4)^{n-1}>0.
\]

For completeness, we also justify the converse approximation for every $b\in(0,1)$. Suppose that $x\in\operatorname{spt}\mu$ and, along a subsequence, a connected neighbourhood $B\cap\overline\Omega$ of $x$ contains no point with $|u_i|\leq1-b$. Passing to a further subsequence, assume $u_i>1-b$ there. The energy bound implies $u_i\to1$ in measure and in $L^1$ on this neighbourhood. Choose a $C^1$ function $h$ on $[1-b,C_0]$, with bounded derivative, satisfying
\[
h(1)=0,\qquad 2W h'+W'h=2W.
\]
Such a function extends regularly across $1$: writing $W(s)=(s-1)^2w(s)$, with $w>0$, it is given by
\[
h(s)=\frac{\displaystyle\int_1^s(q-1)\sqrt{w(q)}\,dq}
{(s-1)\sqrt{w(s)}},\qquad h(1)=0.
\]
For a nonnegative smooth cutoff $\psi$ supported in $B$, test \eqref{equation: EL} with $\psi h(u_i)$ to obtain
\[
\int_\Omega\psi\left(\frac{2W(u_i)}{\varepsilon_i}
+2h_0h'(u_i)\xi_{\varepsilon_i}(u_i)\right)\,d\mathrm{vol}_g
=\lambda_i\int_\Omega\psi h(u_i)\,d\mathrm{vol}_g
-\varepsilon_i\int_\Omega h(u_i)\nabla u_i\cdot\nabla\psi\,d\mathrm{vol}_g.
\]
The right-hand side tends to zero: its first term does so by $h(u_i)\to0$ in $L^1$, and its second is $O(\sqrt{\varepsilon_i})$ by the energy bound. By \cite[Theorem 5.4]{LiPariseSarnataro2023}, $\xi_{\varepsilon_i}(u_i)\to0$ in $L^1_{\mathrm{loc}}(\overline\Omega\cap U)$. It follows that $\int\psi W(u_i)/\varepsilon_i\to0$, and hence $\int\psi e_{\varepsilon_i}(u_i)\to0$. Taking $\psi=1$ near $x$ contradicts $x\in\operatorname{spt}\mu$. The negative phase is treated in the same way, using a function $h$ vanishing at $-1$. This proves the converse approximation. Together with the preceding argument and local compactness, it proves Proposition \ref{P:reflected-Hausdorff}.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{lem:app-first-variation}
There is a constant $C$, independent of $i$, such that
\[
 \int_{\partial \Omega \cap U}e_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i})\,d \mathcal{H}^{n - 1} \le C E_0, \qquad \int |B_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}|^2 \, d\mu_{u_{\varepsilon_i}} \le C E_0, \qquad \|\delta\widetilde V_i\|(\widetilde M)\le C E_0.
\]
                                                                                                                
where $\widetilde{V}^{u_{\varepsilon_i}} \in \mathbf{V}_{n-1}(U)$ is defined by (c.f. Remark \ref{R:varifold-definition})
\[ \widetilde{V}^{u_{\varepsilon_i}}(\phi)=\int_{\{\nabla u_{\varepsilon_i} \neq 0\}} \sqrt{2W(u_{\varepsilon_i})} |\nabla u_{\varepsilon_i}| \phi(x,S^{u_{\varepsilon_i}}) \, dv_g. \]
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
The stress-energy tensor $T_i:=e_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i}) g-\varepsilon_i\,du_i\otimes du_i$ satisfies $\operatorname{div}_gT_i=0,$ and $T_i(\nu,\cdot)=e_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i})\,g(\nu,\cdot)$ on $\partial \Omega \cap U$. Consequently, for every $X \in \mathfrak{X}_c(U)$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:app-stress}
 \int_{\Omega \cap U} T_i:\nabla X\, dv_g = \int_{\partial \Omega \cap U} e_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i}) \langle X,\nu \rangle\,d\mathcal{H}^{n - 1}.
\end{equation}
Choosing a fixed $X$ with $X=\nu$ on $\partial \Omega \cap U$, since $|T_i|\le C e_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i})$, there holds (cf. \cite[Lemma~3.2]{LiPariseSarnataro2023})
\begin{equation}\label{eq:app-boundary-energy}
 \int_{\partial \Omega \cap U} e_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i}) \, d \mathcal{H}^{n - 1} = \int_{\Omega \cap U} T_i:\nabla X \, dv_g \le C E_0.
\end{equation}

We next give the isotropic boundary counterpart of \cite[Theorem~6.4]{DRP25}. Set $w_i=|\nabla u_{\varepsilon_i}|$. Arguing as in the proof of Proposition \ref{P:ep-stability}, yields
\begin{align}\label{eq:app-bochner}
 \int_{\Omega \cap U} \varepsilon_i \bigl(|\nabla^2u_{\varepsilon_i}|^2-|\nabla w_i|^2\bigr)\, dv_g \le C E_0, 
\end{align}
where the boundary term is controlled by \eqref{eq:app-boundary-energy}; note that no sign assumption on the second fundamental form of $\partial \Omega$ is required. On $\{w_i>0\}$,
\[
 |\nabla^2u_{\varepsilon_i}|^2-|\nabla w_i|^2 = w_i^2|B_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}|^2+|\nabla^T w_i|^2,
\]
where $\nabla^T$ denotes the tangential gradient along the level sets of $u_{\varepsilon_i}$, which proves the curvature estimate.

For almost every $s$, the regular level set $\{u_{\varepsilon_i}=s\}$ meets $\partial \Omega$ orthogonally, with outward conormal $\nu$, or it does not meet it at all, in which case part of the argument below simplifies. Its first variation is bounded as follows
\begin{align*}
 h_0&\|\delta \widetilde{V}^{u_{\varepsilon_i}}\|(U) \\
 &\le \sqrt{n - 1} \left(\int_{\Omega \cap U} \varepsilon_i|B_{u_{\varepsilon_i}}|^2 w_i^2\, dv_g \right)^{1/2} \left(\int_{\Omega \cap U} \frac{2W(u_{\varepsilon_i})}{\varepsilon_i}\, dv_g \right)^{1/2} + \int_{\partial \Omega \cap U} \sqrt{2W(u_{\varepsilon_i})}\,w_i\,d\mathcal{H}^{n - 1} \\
 & \le C E_0.
\end{align*}
This is the analogue of \cite[Corollary~6.6]{DRP25}, with the additional boundary contribution. In particular, the estimate concerns the full ambient first variation, not only vector fields tangent to the boundary.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- proposition-8: body_md -->
\label{prop:app-rectifiability}
The varifold $V$ is rectifiable and stationary with free boundary:
\[
 \delta V(X)=0 \quad\text{for every }X\in \mathfrak{X}^{tan}_c(U).
\]
<!-- end proposition-8 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
We adapt the positive-density argument of \cite[Theorem~6.7]{DRP25}. First, the boundary version of \cite[Lemma~6.2]{DRP25} gives, for each sufficiently small $b>0$, constants $R,c_b>0$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:app-microscopic-density}
 |u_{\varepsilon_i}(x)|\le 1- b \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \|\widetilde{V}^{u_{\varepsilon_i}}\|(B_{R\varepsilon_i}(x))
 \ge c_b\varepsilon_i^{n - 1}
\end{equation}
for all large $i$. To see this, suppose that the normalised masses on the left tend to zero and rescale by $\varepsilon_i$ about the corresponding points. Elliptic estimates give a limit on $B_R(0)$ intersected with a full space or a half-space containing the origin. This limit is then a constant. Its value lies in $[-1+b,1-b]$, and the equation forces it to equal $\gamma$ (the unique local non-degenerate maxima of $W$). Stability would then imply
\[
 -W''(\gamma)\int\zeta^2\le\int|\nabla\zeta|^2
\]
for every test function supported away from the curved boundary, with no restriction on its trace on the flat boundary. A radial cutoff contradicts this inequality when $R$ is sufficiently large.

Multiplying \eqref{E:Allen-Cahn} with $\gamma - u_{\varepsilon_i}$ gives
\[
 \int_{\Omega \cap U} \frac{(\gamma-u_{\varepsilon_i})W'(u_{\varepsilon_i})}{\varepsilon_i}\,dv_g =\int_{\Omega \cap U} \varepsilon_i |\nabla u_{\varepsilon_i}|^2 \, dv_g \le 2E_0.
\]
The integrand on the left is nonnegative, and $W(s)\le Cb(\gamma-s)W'(s)$ for $|s|>1-b$. Using $h_0 e_{\varepsilon_i} (u_{\varepsilon_i})=2W(u_{\varepsilon_i})/\varepsilon_i+ h_0 \xi_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i})$ therefore gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:app-well-energy}
\int_{\{|u_{\varepsilon_i}|>1-b\}} e_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i}) \, dv_g \le Cb+\int_{\Omega \cap U} |\xi_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i})|\, dv_g.
\end{equation}
Compare \cite[equation~(6.12)]{DRP25}.

Fix a sufficiently small $r_0>0$ and define
\[
 G_{i,b}:=\left\{x\in \Omega \cap U:
 \begin{array}{l}
 |u_{\varepsilon_i}(x)|\le1-b,\\
 \|\delta\widetilde{V}^{u_{\varepsilon_i}}\|(B_r(x)) \le b^{-1}\|\widetilde{V}^{u_{\varepsilon_i}}\|(B_r(x)) \quad\text{for every }0<r<r_0
 \end{array}\right\}.
\]
Besicovitch's covering theorem, Lemma~\ref{lem:app-first-variation}, and \eqref{eq:app-well-energy} imply
\begin{equation}\label{eq:app-good-mass}
 \|\widetilde{V}^{u_{\varepsilon_i}}\|(M\setminus G_{i,b}) \leq Cb + o_i(1).
\end{equation}
This is the good-set construction in \cite[equation~(6.11) and the proof of Theorem~6.7]{DRP25}. At a point of $G_{i,b}$, the first-variation bound in its definition and the varifold monotonicity inequality propagate \eqref{eq:app-microscopic-density} to
\begin{equation}\label{eq:app-propagated-density}
 \|\widetilde{V}^{u_{\varepsilon_i}}\|(B_r(x))\ge c_b r^{n - 1} \qquad(R\varepsilon_i\le r<r_0).
\end{equation}
The exponential factor absorbs both $b^{-1}$ and the ambient geometric error. Since we have bounded the full first variation in $U$, this applies also when the ball intersects $\partial \Omega$.

For fixed $b$, take a weak limit $\sigma_b$ of $\|\widetilde{V}^{u_{\varepsilon_i}}\| \mathbin{\vrule height 1.6ex depth 0pt width
0.13ex\vrule height 0.13ex depth 0pt width 1.3ex} G_{i,b}$. Then $\sigma_b\le\mu$ and $(\mu-\sigma_b)(U)\le Cb$. Every point of $\operatorname{spt}\sigma_b$ is a limit of points in $G_{i,b}$, so \eqref{eq:app-propagated-density}, applied first on slightly smaller balls, gives $\mu(B_r(x))\ge c_b r^{n - 1}$ there. Letting $b$ tend to zero along a countable sequence proves positive upper $(n - 1)$-density at $\mu$-almost every point. Lemma~\ref{lem:app-first-variation} and Allard's rectifiability theorem \cite[Section~5]{Allard1972} now show that $V$ is rectifiable.

Finally, $h_0 e_{\varepsilon_i} (u_{\varepsilon_i}) - \sqrt{2W(u_{\varepsilon_i})} \vert \nabla u_{\varepsilon_i} \vert \leq h_0 \vert \xi_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i}) \vert$ combined with $|\varepsilon_i|\nabla u_{\varepsilon_i}|^2- \sqrt{2W(u_{\varepsilon_i})} \vert \nabla u_{\varepsilon_i} \vert|\le 2 h_0 |\xi_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i})|$ imply that $h_0^{-1}T_i\,dv_g$ converges. For $X \in \mathfrak{X}^{tan}_c(U)$, the right-hand side of \eqref{eq:app-stress} vanishes. Passing to the limit proves $\delta V(X)=0$.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- proposition-9: body_md -->
\label{prop:app-integrality}
The varifold $V$ is integral, including its restriction to $\partial \Omega$ (compare with \cite[Proposition~6.1]{LiPariseSarnataro2023}).
<!-- end proposition-9 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
We follow  \cite[Lemmas~7.7--7.8 and Theorem~7.10]{DRP25}. The fact that $\xi_{\varepsilon_i}(u_{\varepsilon_i}) \rightarrow 0$ in $L^1$ simplifies the proof. 

Fix a point $x$ at which $V$ has density $\theta\in(0,\infty)$ and a unique tangent varifold $\theta|P|$. At $\mu$-almost every such point in $\partial \Omega \cap U$, arguing as in \cite[Proposition~6.1]{LiPariseSarnataro2023} it is enough to consider $P=T_x\partial \Omega$. Using normal coordinates in the interior and Fermi coordinates at the boundary, choose a diagonal blow-up sequence with $\varepsilon_i/r_i\to0$. The resulting metrics $g_i$ converge smoothly to the Euclidean metric, and the domains are either a full space or the half-space $\{t\ge0\}$. Write $z=(y,t)\in\mathbb R^{n - 1}\times\mathbb R$, with $P=\{t=0\}$, and set
\[
 I_r:=(-r,r)\quad\text{or}\quad I_r:=[0,r), \qquad Q_r:=B_r^{n - 1}\times I_r,
\]
respectively. In the boundary case $\partial_tu_{\varepsilon_i}=0$ at $t=0$. From now on, write
\[
 \bar e_i:=\frac{\varepsilon_i}{2}|Du_{\varepsilon_i}|^2 + \frac{W(u_{\varepsilon_i})}{\varepsilon_i}, \qquad \bar\xi_i:=\frac{\varepsilon_i}{2}|Du_{\varepsilon_i}|^2 - \frac{W(u_{\varepsilon_i})}{\varepsilon_i}
\]
for the Euclidean energy and discrepancy in these coordinates. The metric errors vanish, and varifold convergence together with vanishing of the discrepancy in $L^1$ yields
\begin{equation}\label{eq:app-flat-limit}
 h_0^{-1}\bar e_i\,dz\rightharpoonup \theta\,\mathcal H^{n - 1} \mathbin{\vrule height 1.6ex depth 0pt width
0.13ex\vrule height 0.13ex depth 0pt width 1.3ex} P, \qquad \int_{Q_3}\bigl(|\bar\xi_i|+ \varepsilon_i|D_yu_{\varepsilon_i}|^2\bigr)\,dz\longrightarrow0.
\end{equation}
The second assertion is the isotropic counterpart of \cite[equation~(7.12)]{DRP25}, with the discrepancy included.

Choose $0\le\chi\in C_c^\infty((-1,1))$ with $\chi=1$ on $[-1/2,1/2]$, and define
\[
 f_i(y):=\frac1{h_0}\int_{I_1}\chi(t)\bar e_i(y,t)\,dt.
\]
As in \cite[Lemma~7.7]{DRP25}, test the stress-energy identity with $X=\chi(t)\varphi(y)\partial_{y_a}$. The resulting distributional identity on $B_2^{n - 1}$ has the form
\begin{equation}\label{eq:app-projected-stress}
 Df_i=\operatorname{div}R_i+r_i,
 \qquad \|R_i\|_{L^1(B_2^{n - 1})}+\|r_i\|_{L^1(B_2^{n - 1})}\rightarrow0.
\end{equation}
In the Euclidean metric, the terms are explicit
\[
 R_i^{ab}=\frac1{h_0}\int_{I_1}\chi\varepsilon_i
                  \partial_{y_a}u_i\partial_{y_b}u_i\,dt,
 \qquad
 r_i^a=-\frac1{h_0}\int_{I_1}\chi'\varepsilon_i
                  \partial_{y_a}u_i\partial_tu_i\,dt.
\]
Their $L^1$ norms tend to zero by \eqref{eq:app-flat-limit} and Cauchy--Schwarz. The additional tensor and zeroth-order terms are bounded by $C\|g_i-\delta\|_{C^1}\int_{Q_3}\bar e_i$. Since $f_i\ge0$ and $f_i\,dy\rightharpoonup\theta\,dy$, Allard's strong constancy lemma \cite[Theorem~1.(4)]{Allard86} gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:app-strong-constancy}
 f_i\rightarrow\theta\quad\text{in }L^1(B_1^{n - 1}).
\end{equation}
There holds
\begin{equation}\label{eq:app-local-wells}
 \int_{Q_2\cap\{|u_i|>1-b\}}\bar e_i\,dz
 \le Cb+o_i(1).
\end{equation}
Put
\[
 d_i(y):=\int_{I_2}\bigl(|\bar\xi_i|+
                    \varepsilon_i|D_yu_{\varepsilon_i}|^2\bigr)(y,t)\,dt.
\]
The weak-$(1,1)$ estimate for the local Hardy--Littlewood maximal operator $\mathcal M$ and \eqref{eq:app-flat-limit} show that $\mathcal M d_i\to0$ in measure on $B_1^{n - 1}$. For fixed $b>0$, we can therefore choose Lebesgue points $y_i\in B_1^{n - 1}$ such that
\begin{align}\label{eq:app-good-slice}
 f_i(y_i)&\rightarrow\theta,
 &\mathcal M d_i(y_i)&\rightarrow0,\notag\\
 \int_{I_2\cap\{|t|\ge1/4\}}\bar e_i(y_i,t)\,dt
 &\rightarrow0,
 &\int_{I_2\cap\{|u_{\varepsilon_i}(y_i,\cdot)|>1-b\}}
       \bar e_i(y_i,t)\,dt&\le Cb+o_i(1).
\end{align}
For the third condition use the concentration of the limit measure on $P$. For the last one use \eqref{eq:app-local-wells} and Markov's inequality, increasing $C$ so that its exceptional set occupies at most half of $B_1^{n - 1}$. The exceptional sets for the other conditions have vanishing measure. This makes explicit the selection used in \cite[proof of Theorem~7.10]{DRP25}. In particular,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:app-total-slice}
 \int_{I_1}\bar e_i(y_i,t)\,dt\rightarrow h_0\theta,
 \qquad \text{and} \qquad d_i(y_i)\rightarrow0.
\end{equation}

Consider any sequence $t_i\in I_1$ such that
$|u_{\varepsilon_i}(y_i,t_i)|\le1-b$, and rescale about $(y_i,t_i)$ by $\varepsilon_i$. For each fixed $R$, the definition of the maximal operator gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:app-microscopic-defect}
 \varepsilon_i^{-(n - 1)}\int_{B_{R\varepsilon_i}(y_i,t_i)\cap Q_2}
 \bigl(|\bar\xi_i|+\varepsilon_i|D_yu_{\varepsilon_i}|^2\bigr)\,dz
 \le C R^{n - 1}\mathcal M d_i(y_i)\rightarrow0.
\end{equation} 
Compactness therefore gives a one-dimensional limit $q=q(t)$ satisfying (cf. \cite[Lemma~7.8 and Remark~7.9]{DRP25})
\begin{equation}\label{eq:app-profile}
 q''=W'(q),\qquad \frac12(q')^2=W(q),\qquad |q(0)|\le1-b.
\end{equation}

In the boundary case, if $t_i/\varepsilon_i$ remained bounded, the limit would be defined on a half-line $[-b,\infty)$ and satisfy $q'(-b)=0$. Equation~\eqref{eq:app-profile} would force $q(-b)=\pm1$, and uniqueness for the ordinary differential equation would make $q$ constant, a contradiction. Moreover, another contradiction argument using \eqref{eq:app-profile} gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:app-transversality}
 \varepsilon_i|\partial_tu_{\varepsilon_i}(y_i,t)|\ge c_b>0
 \quad\text{whenever }t\in I_1,
 \quad |u_{\varepsilon_i}(y_i,t)|\le1-b,
\end{equation}
for all large $i$.

The uniform estimate $\varepsilon_i|Du_{\varepsilon_i}|\le C$ on $Q_2$ and the third condition in \eqref{eq:app-good-slice} exclude transition points near the artificial endpoints of $I_1$. Together with the preceding boundary argument, this shows that every component of
\[
 \{t\in I_1:|u_{\varepsilon_i}(y_i,t)|<1-b\}
\]
has both endpoints in the interior of $I_1$. By \eqref{eq:app-transversality}, the restriction of $u_i$ to each component is strictly monotone, and its endpoint values are $-1+b$ and $1-b$, in either order.

We can now prove integrality. Let $N_i$ be the number of these components and set
\[
 h_b:=\int_{-1+b}^{1-b}\sqrt{2W(s)}\,ds.
\]
The elementary inequality
\begin{equation}\label{eq:app-slice-comparison}
 0\le\bar e_i-\sqrt{2W(u_{\varepsilon_i})}\,|\partial_t u_{\varepsilon_i}|
 \le|\bar\xi_i|+\varepsilon_i|D_yu_{\varepsilon_i}|^2
\end{equation}
shows, by change of variables on each monotone component and \eqref{eq:app-total-slice}, that
\[
 \int_{I_1\cap\{|u_{\varepsilon_i}(y_i,\cdot)|<1-b\}}\bar e_i(y_i,t)\,dt =N_i h_b+o_i(1).
\]
In particular, $N_i$ is bounded independently of $i$ and of sufficiently small $b$. The remaining energy is bounded by the last condition in \eqref{eq:app-good-slice}. Since $h_b=h_0+O(b^2)$, we obtain $ \operatorname{dist}(\theta,\mathbb N)\le Cb.$ Letting $b \downarrow0$ proves $\theta\in\mathbb N$ and completes the argument.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- definition-8: body_md -->
Let $X$ be a locally compact Hausdorff space and $N\in \mathbb{N}$. Let $\mu$ be a Radon measure on $X$ and $f:X\to\mathbb{R}^N$ a Borel-measurable vector-valued function which is in $L^1_{loc}(X;\mu)$. We shall call such a pair $(\mu,f)$ a \textit{measure-function pair} over $X$.
<!-- end definition-8 -->
<!-- definition-9: body_md -->
Let $(\mu_i, f_i)_{i\in\mathbb{N}}$ be a sequence of measure-function pairs over $X$. We say $(\mu_i, f_i)$ \textit{converges} to a measure function pair $(\mu,f)$ over $X$ in the \textit{weak sense} (or, more simply, \textit{weakly}) if the following conditions hold:
\begin{enumerate}[(i)]
    \item $\mu_i \to \mu$ as Radon measures on $X$; and
    \item for any $\phi \in C_c(X,\mathbb{R}^N)$, we have
    \begin{equation}
        \int_X\langle f_i, \phi\rangle\,d\mu_i\to\int_X\langle f,\phi\rangle\, d\mu.
    \end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
We shall denote this convergence by $(\mu_i, f_i)\rightharpoonup(\mu, f)$.
<!-- end definition-9 -->
<!-- proposition-10: body_md -->
\label{prop:measure-function compactness}
Let $(\mu_i, f_i)_{i\in\mathbb{N}}$ be a sequence of measure-function pairs over $X$. Suppose that
\begin{equation}
    \sup_i \mu_i(X)<+\infty,
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}
    \sup_i\int_X|f_i|^2\,d\mu_i<+\infty.
\end{equation}
Then, there is a subsequence $(\mu_{i'}, f_{i'})$ and a measure-function pair $(\mu, f)$ over $X$ such that
\begin{equation}
    (\mu_{i'}, f_{i'})\rightharpoonup(\mu,f).
\end{equation}
Moreover, the weak limit $(\mu, f)$ satisfies
\begin{equation}
\label{E:lower-semicontinuity-measure-function}
    \int_X|f|^2\, d\mu\leq\liminf_{i}\int_X|f_i|^2\, d\mu_i.
\end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-10 -->
